Only the legend at `quanlydonhang.fxml` line 190 says "Trống". To a new cashier "không hoạt động" reads as "out of service", not "free to seat someone". **(confirmed in code; the wording may be the owner's choice, so ask first)** Pick one word (the code already knows "Trống"), keep it in `TableStatus` only and let every screen read it from there, so the next wording change is one line.

69. [ ] **[Low] Importing products from a CSV file is not all-or-nothing.** **NEW (10th scan)** In `ProductController` (`AdminController` before the 13th-pass split) the confirmed import runs `for (Product product : toAdd) productDAO.insert(product)`, and `ProductDAO.insert` opens its own connection and commits by itself. If row 40 of 100 fails (the disk fills, the file is locked for more than the 5 s timeout, the MariaDB server drops), rows 1-39 stay in the menu, the dialog shows an error, and a second try reports those 39 as already existing. It is also one connection per row, slow over a network. **(confirmed in code; not reproduced, because the failure needs an injected fault)** Add `ProductDAO.insertAll(List<Product>)` that inserts in one transaction (like `OrderDAO` does for an order), call it from the import, and test with a duplicate that makes the last row fail: nothing may be added.

70. [ ] **[Med] At the POS a customer attached with "Gắn khách hàng" who has no phone stays attached when a different phone is typed, so the sale is paid under the wrong person and the new customer is never created.** **NEW (11th scan)** The phone box's listener (`OrderController.initCustomerInputs`) detaches the attached customer only when `attachedCustomer.getPhone() != null` and differs from what is typed. `handleEditOrder` offers every customer, including those saved without a phone (the phone is optional), and fills the phone box with "". The cashier then types the real customer's number: if that number is known, `lookupCustomerByPhone` swaps the customer (fine); if it is new, the label says "Khách mới: sẽ được lưu khi gọi món hoặc thanh toán", but `CheckoutService.pay` uses `knownCustomer` first, so the points go to the customer picked in the dialog and no new customer is saved. Editing the name box never detaches either. **(confirmed in code; not run, the screen cannot be loaded here)** Detach (and re-run the lookup) whenever the phone or the name box changes and no longer matches the attached customer, also when that customer has no phone; add a test for the rule once it sits in a service (6.5).

71. [ ] **[Low] `DatabaseConfig.getConnection` leaks the connection when the set-up after connecting fails.** **NEW (11th scan)** On SQLite it opens the connection, then runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, `busy_timeout`, `synchronous` and registers the `VN_KEY` function; none of that is inside a `try` that closes the connection. If one of those calls throws (the file is locked longer than the timeout, a disk I/O error, a damaged file), the exception reaches the caller but the open connection (and its file handle, and in WAL mode its share of the `-shm` file) stays until the garbage collector finds it. On a shop PC that retries every 30 s (`SessionGuard`), a long fault piles up open handles, and on Windows an open handle blocks a backup restore or a file delete. **(confirmed in code)** Wrap the set-up in `try { ... } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) { connection.close(); throw e; }`; test with a connection source that fails on the pragma.

72. [ ] **[Low] The silent password-hash upgrade at login also clears "must change password".** **NEW (11th scan)** `AuthenticationService.upgradeHashIfOld` calls `UserDAO.updatePasswordHash`, which is the method the person's own password change uses, and it writes `must_change_password = 0` (comment: "any must-change mark is over"). That is right for a chosen password, wrong for a re-hash: an account whose password an administrator just reset (flag 1) and whose hash has fewer iterations than `PasswordUtil.ITERATIONS` would log in once, have the hash upgraded and the flag cleared in the same call, and never be asked to choose its own password (2A.18). Today an admin-set hash is always made with the current count, so this is latent, and it becomes real the next time `ITERATIONS` is raised (the count was raised once already, 2.12). **(confirmed in code)** Give the re-hash its own method (`UPDATE users SET password_hash = ? WHERE id = ?`), keep `updatePasswordHash` for a chosen password, and add a test: flag 1 + old hash, log in, flag is still 1.

73. [ ] **[Low] The 180-second limit on `mysqldump` never applies, so a stuck dump freezes the backup for ever.** **NEW (11th scan)** `MariaDbBackup.dumpTo` reads the program's output with `in.readNBytes(...)` and `in.transferTo(...)` (both block until the program closes its output, i.e. until it ends) and only afterwards calls `process.waitFor(TIMEOUT_SECONDS, ...)`. If `mysqldump` hangs (a table lock that never frees, a network stall to the server on another PC), the thread never reaches the timeout: the dashboard's backup button stays disabled, and an automatic backup thread sits there. **(confirmed in code; a hang needs a fault to reproduce)** Start a small reader thread for the output (or redirect it to a file), call `waitFor(timeout)` on the main thread, and `destroyForcibly()` on a timeout; test with a fake `db.mysqldump` script that sleeps.

74. [ ] **[Med] The MariaDB backup probably fails on MySQL 8 with the user that `docs/DATABASE.md` tells the owner to create.** **NEW (11th scan, inferred)** The guide grants `GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON lunavera.*` (database level only) and the app accepts `db.type=mysql`. `mysqldump` from MySQL 8.0.21 and later also dumps tablespace information, which needs the global `PROCESS` privilege that a database-level grant does not include, so it stops with "Access denied; you need (at least one of) the PROCESS privilege(s)". `MariaDbBackup.command` does not pass `--no-tablespaces` (MariaDB's own client ignores the need, so XAMPP/MariaDB shops are not affected, and that is the shop the guide describes). **(reasoned from how mysqldump 8 behaves; not run, no MySQL 8 here)** Add `--no-tablespaces` to the command (MariaDB's `mysqldump` ignores it only from 10.? versions: check the option exists in the shop's client, or retry without it when the error says the option is unknown), and add a line to the troubleshooting table of `docs/DATABASE.md`.

75. [ ] **[Low] Buttons that start background work stay clickable while the work runs.** **NEW (12th scan)** Since the 13th pass the delete-category, delete-product, lock-account and restore flows run in the background (`Async.run`), but only a few buttons are disabled meanwhile: counting `Async.run` against `setDisable(true)` per controller gives `ProductController` 6 / 0, `CategoryController` 3 / 0, `AdminDashboardController` 9 / 1 (only the backup button), `EmployeeController` 2 / 0, `LoginController` 2 / 0. A quick second click on "Xóa" or "Khóa" opens a second confirmation (or starts the same action twice; the delete of a category is safe because `deleteIfUnused` is atomic, the second "Khôi phục" is not obviously so). **(confirmed by the counts and by reading `CategoryController.deleteCategory`; not reproduced on a screen)** Give `Async.run` an optional `Node... busy` argument that disables those nodes until the callback ends (in `finally`, so an error re-enables them), use it in every background action, and add a test of that helper with a fake toolkit-free runner. Related: 2.65 (the payment button), 5.16 (a busy indicator).

76. [ ] **[Low] The category dialog changes the table's own row before the save has worked, and a refused save leaves the refused text on screen.** **NEW (14th scan)** `CategoryDialog.show` takes `existing` (the very `Category` object the table shows) and calls `setName`, `setDescription` and `setActive` on it first, then `CATEGORIES.update(category)`. If the database refuses (the name is taken, the connection dropped) the dialog stays open, which is right, but when the person then presses Cancel nothing reloads (`afterSave` runs only after a save), so the row keeps the refused name until the next reload, the next edit opens with it, and the table says something the database does not. The product dialog does the same on `existing` but always calls `reloadProducts()` afterwards, so only the category screen shows the problem. **(confirmed in code)** Edit a copy (`new Category(existing)`) and copy the values back only after a successful save, or reload in every case.
77. [ ] **[Med] A decimal amount typed in a money box is read as a number 10 or 100 times too big.** **NEW (15th scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` (2.16) drops every `.`, `,` and space wherever it sits, so it cannot tell a thousands separator from a decimal point. **Reproduced on this tree:** `25.5` and `25,5` give 255, `10.50` gives 1050, `1,234.5` gives 12345, `25,000.50` gives 2500050, `1.000,5` gives 10005, `1,2,3` gives 123. `parseDong` and `parseOptionalDong` serve the product price and cost, the discount, the cash received, the salary and the CSV import, so a price typed `25.5` (for 25.500) or a pasted `10.50` is saved without a word, 10 to 100 times too small or too large. 3.46 describes the same flaw only for the CSV stock column. Fix: accept a separator only when exactly three digits follow it (`25.000`, `1,250,000`); anything else with a `.` or `,` is refused with the existing message plus "không nhập số thập phân". Add the cases above to `MoneyParserTest`.
78. [ ] **[Med] When the disk is full, every sale fails with a message that says "try again".** **NEW (15th scan)** **Reproduced** on a 14 MB filesystem filled to the brim: 400 of 400 sales failed (nothing half-saved: no order, stock and points unchanged), with the cause `SQLITE_IOERR_SHMSIZE`. `DataAccessException.hintFor` knows a wrong password, a lost server and a busy database, but not a full disk, so the cashier sees only the generic "Đã xảy ra lỗi. Hãy thử lại; nếu lỗi vẫn còn, báo người phụ trách" and retries again and again. A full disk is handled at start-up (`StartupProblem`) and in the backup (free-space check), nowhere else. Fix: map `SQLITE_FULL`, `SQLITE_IOERR*` and MariaDB 1021/1114 to "Ổ đĩa đầy hoặc không ghi được. Hãy giải phóng dung lượng rồi thử lại; đơn vẫn được giữ trên màn hình"; see 4.24 for a warning before it happens (free space under 200 MB on the data drive).
79. [ ] **[Med] While a payment is being saved, the POS screen stays fully usable, and the result can wipe or mix up what the cashier did meanwhile.** **NEW (15th scan)** `OrderController.completeOrder` disables only `paymentButton` and runs the payment with `Async.runOrdered`. The tables, dish cards, +/−, customer boxes and the sidebar stay live (`orderInProgress` says an order exists, not that one is being paid). If the cashier taps another table or a dish in that moment (a slow MariaDB link makes it seconds), the success callback runs `cart.clear()`, `resetOrderAfterPayment()` and sets `activeOpenOrder = null` on whatever is on screen then, so the other table's lines vanish from the screen while its order still exists in the database, and the next autosave tries to create a second OPEN order for that table (refused by 3.22, shown as an error). **(confirmed in code that nothing blocks the screen; not reproduced, JavaFX is not available here)** Fix: a `paymentInFlight` flag that disables the table map, dish list, cart buttons and sidebar (or an overlay "Đang lưu…") until the callback ends, and have the callback check that `selectedTableNumber` is still the table that was paid. Related: 2.55, 2.65, 2.75, 5.16.

80. [ ] **[Low] A price typed with the real dong sign "₫" is refused.** **NEW (16th scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` strips only the endings `vnđ`, `vnd`, `đ` and `d` (the letter), and every other character is an error. The app itself prints the letter ("12.000đ", `CurrencyUtil`), so copying a price from the app works, but the sign `₫` (U+20AB) that Windows, phone keyboards, bank apps and supplier price lists use is not accepted. **Reproduced by running the class:** `10.000đ` and `10 000₫` are read, `10000₫` and `₫10000` give "Số tiền chỉ được gồm chữ số". It fails in every money box that uses the parser (price, cost, discount, cash received, salary, CSV). **(confirmed by running the code)** Strip a leading or trailing `₫` and the other ending as well, case-insensitively and with `Locale.ROOT` (the parser calls `toLowerCase()` with the computer's locale), and add the cases to `MoneyParserTest`. Related: 2.77.

81. [ ] **[Low] The product CSV import accepts three kinds of questionable row without a word.** **NEW (16th scan)** Reproduced by running `ProductCsv.parse` on sample files: (1) in the "Đang bán" column anything except `không`, `0`, `false` and `no` means "on sale" (`a.equals("khong") || ...` then `setActive(!...)`), so `Ngừng`, `Hết`, `Tạm ngưng`, `n`, `off` or a typo put the product on sale; (2) a cost above the price (`45000` and `90000`) is imported silently, while the product form asks "vẫn lưu?" for the same input (3.19); (3) a row with more cells than the header, or fewer, is read as far as it goes and the rest is ignored. **(confirmed by running the code)** Accept only an explicit list for the active column (`có`, `co`, `1`, `true`, `yes`, `x` and `không`, `0`, `false`, `no`, `ngừng bán`, `ngung ban`); anything else becomes a problem line; show cost-above-price rows as a warning list before the confirmation (the file is still importable); count extra cells as a notice. Tests with the rows above. Related: 2.69, 3.46.

82. [ ] **[Med] A database password with a backslash, a space at either end, or `\u` in it is silently changed, and a password ending in a backslash swallows the next setting.** **NEW (19th scan)** `DatabaseSettings.load` reads `data/database.properties` with `Properties.load` and then `.trim()`s every value. Run on the real class: `db.password=ab\cd` is used as `abcd`; `ab\\cd` as `ab\cd`; `x\u0041y` as `xAy`; `  lead` and `trail   ` lose their spaces; and `abc\` (a backslash at the end of the line) joins the next line, so the password becomes `abcdb.type=sqlite` and the `db.type=sqlite` setting under it is lost, which sends a SQLite shop to MariaDB. Characters like `#`, `!`, `=` and `:` are fine. The shop sees only "Access denied" or "cannot connect", with the password written correctly in the file. A password made by a generator, as `docs/DATABASE.md` advises ("use a long one"), can easily contain a backslash. **(reproduced by running `DatabaseSettings.fromProperties`)** Fix: read the file in a way that has no escapes (read the lines yourself, split at the first `=`, and trim only a trailing line break), or at least refuse a value that contains `\` with a message, and write in `docs/DATABASE.md` and `database.properties.example` that the password must not contain a backslash; add a test with the nine cases above.

83. [ ] **[Low] A printer driver that throws leaves the print job open, and printing blocks the screen.** **NEW (22nd scan)** Item 2.14 handled a `printPage` that returns false. In `HoaDonController.handlePrint` the call sits in `try { printed = job.printPage(toPrint); } finally { remove the transform }` with no `catch`: a `RuntimeException` from the driver (a printer that went offline, a network roll printer that times out) skips `endJob`/`cancelJob`, so the job and its queue entry stay open and the cashier gets no message except the generic crash dialog. The whole call, including `showPrintDialog`, also runs on the screen thread, so a slow network printer freezes the till until it answers. The method also reads `job.getJobSettings().getPageLayout()` twice in a row (a leftover line). **(confirmed in code; a faulty printer is needed to see it)** Catch `RuntimeException` around `printPage`, call `cancelJob()` and show "Không thể in" with the reason; print on a background thread (printing may be started off the FX thread) and re-enable the button afterwards.

84. [ ] **[Med] Opening an old customer-role account in the account dialog and pressing "Lưu" turns it into a staff account.** **NEW (24th scan)** `AccountController.openAccountDialog` fills the role box with `existing.getRole()` only when it is not `CUSTOMER`; otherwise it shows **Nhân viên**, and the box offers only Admin and Employee. The save then calls `accountService.update`, which writes `form.role()` through `changeRoleAndStatus`. The account list still shows these accounts (the role filter has "Khách hàng"; item 2A.23 stopped them logging in but they are still in the table), so an administrator who opens one to fix a typo or to lock it and presses Save has given that customer login a staff role, and a later login with it is a cashier's. The dialog never says the role changed, and the security log line says only "role CUSTOMER -> EMPLOYEE". **(confirmed in code; the screen was not run)** Keep the role unchanged when the account is a `CUSTOMER` one (show it read-only, or hide the box), and make `AccountService.update` refuse a change of role away from `CUSTOMER` unless an explicit action asks for it; add the case to `AccountServiceTest`.

85. [ ] **[Med] After a crash, a restore's safety copy (and the restore itself) can lose the last sales that were still in the write-ahead log.** **NEW (25th scan)** The database runs in WAL mode (6.16): committed sales sit in `lunavera.db-wal` until a checkpoint moves them into `lunavera.db`, and a power cut or a killed process leaves the `-wal` file behind. A restore that was parked before the crash is applied in `LunaveraApplication.init()` *before* the database is opened, so nothing has recovered the log yet: `BackupService.applyPendingRestore` writes the "before-restore" safety zip with `writeZip(safety, database, ...)`, which copies the bare `lunavera.db` file only (the `-wal` is not included), and then deletes `-journal`, `-wal` and `-shm` before moving the restored file in. Everything sold since the last checkpoint is therefore missing from the safety copy and is gone from the shop's disk, although the message promises "Dữ liệu trước đó được lưu tại ...". **(confirmed in code; the crash itself was not staged)** Before the safety copy, open the live file once (SQLite replays the log on the first connection) and take it with `VACUUM INTO` (the way `DatabaseConfig.backupTo` does) or run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` and close; only then delete the side files. Test: write a row, copy the db and `-wal` pair to a folder without checkpointing, run the restore, and look for the row inside the safety zip.

## 2A. Security flaws  **NEW (4th scan)**
This is a local desktop app with one till, so the main risks are a curious or careless person at the till, a lost computer or USB stick, and mistakes that lock the shop out. Related items found earlier:
bug 2.2 (default admin password stays usable), bug 2.3 (unlimited discounts), bug 2.12 (password hash strength), edge case 3.8 (any employee can empty a table) and feature 4.2 (audit log).

1. [x] **[Med] CSV export can run a formula in Excel.** `ReportWindow.csv()` only quotes commas, quotes and line breaks. A product named `=HYPERLINK("http://...")` or starting with `+`, `-` or `@` becomes a live formula when the report is opened in Excel. **DONE in code, session 43:** CSV cells that start with `= + - @`, a tab or a carriage return get a leading apostrophe (`Csv.cell`; used by the report export; tested).
       Prefix such cells with an apostrophe or a space (and treat a leading tab or carriage return the same way). Add a test. **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `csv()` returned `=HYPERLINK(...)`, `+1+1`, `@SUM(A1)` and `-2+3` unchanged (only the first got quotes, from its inner double quotes); a value with a bare carriage return (`a\rb`) is not quoted either.
2. [x] **[Med] The "last admin" rule can be beaten.** The check (`countActiveAdmins() <= 1`) lives in the screen, then `UserDAO.updateStatus` / `updateRole` run as a separate step with no guard. Two admins working at once can lock or demote each other and leave no active admin, *(DONE in code, session 42: the rule is one UPDATE inside `AccountService`, checked by the database in the same statement; tested with the last admin.)*
       and the only way back in is editing the database by hand. Enforce it in the DAO in one transaction (for example `UPDATE ... WHERE (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users WHERE role='ADMIN' AND status='ACTIVE') > 1`) and test it. **(confirmed in code)**
3. [ ] **[Med] Permissions are checked only in the screens.** `requireAdmin()` and `Session.isAdmin()` sit in controllers; `OrderDAO.cancelPaidOrder`, `UserDAO.updateRole`, `updatePasswordHash`, `ProductDAO.update` and the backup code check nothing. One forgotten check in a future handler exposes the action. **PARTLY DONE, session 43:** PARTLY: creating, editing and locking accounts, restoring a backup and refunding a paid order now refuse a non-admin inside the service/DAO (`Permissions.requireAdmin`; tested with a cashier and with nobody signed in). Still open, owner's decision: should employees be able to open the accounts list read-only (they see every staff username and phone)? And the permission check for the other DAO methods. *(8th pass, by reading the callers: account creation, editing and locking, restore and refund are enforced below the screen already. `UserDAO.updateStatus` and `updateRole` have no caller in the app at all (the account service writes its own SQL); `updatePasswordHash` is called only by the person's own password change and the login re-hash. Product and category writes are called only from `ProductController`, `CategoryController` and `CategoryDialog` (one `AdminController` before the 13th-pass split), which send a non-admin away in `initialize()` through `AdminScreen.blockNonAdmin`, and since the 13th pass the delete flows also check the permission in `CategoryService` and `ProductService` (so the old question about employees seeing the accounts list is closed: they cannot open it). What is left is putting `Permissions.requireAdmin` into `ProductDAO`/`CategoryDAO`; not done because the existing tests call those DAOs with nobody signed in and would all need a session first, and the tests cannot be run here.)*
       Add a small permission check in the service layer (see 6.5 and 6.21). Also, employees can open the accounts list read-only and see every staff username and phone number; decide whether that is wanted. **(confirmed)**
4. [ ] **[Med] Customer data and password hashes are stored and backed up in plain form.** The database holds customer names and phone numbers and every PBKDF2 hash, and the backup button copies all of it to any folder or USB stick with no warning. **PARTLY DONE, session 43:** PARTLY: the backup-done message now warns that the zip holds customer names, phones and password hashes unencrypted. Not done: a passphrase-protected backup, owner-only folder permissions, and the legal notice (ask an adviser). *(8th pass: on Linux and macOS the data folder is now set to owner-only (`rwx------`) at every start, `DataPaths.restrictToOwner`, tested. Windows has no such call in plain Java and nothing was changed there. The passphrase-protected backup is not done.)*
       Add a warning in the backup dialog, offer a passphrase-protected backup, keep the live database readable only by the shop's Windows user, and note that moving data into the project folder (1.3) makes the exposure larger.
       Customers are never told their phone number is stored and cannot ask for removal (feature 4.8); Vietnam has a personal-data decree, so ask an adviser what notice or consent the shop needs. **(confirmed for the storage; the legal part is for an adviser)**
5. [x] **[Med] The default password is built into the program.** On any empty database `seedDefaultAdmin` created `admin` with the known password `Admin@123`, so deleting or moving `lunavera.db` reset the shop to a known login. **(confirmed)** *(DONE in code, session 44: no admin is created any more on a new database. The first start shows "Thiết lập lần đầu" where the owner picks name, username and a password (8+ characters, letter and digit). "Thoát" closes the app. Old installs that still have `Admin@123` get the forced-change dialog (see 2.2). The dialog has not been seen in a live window yet, see section 0.)*
6. [ ] **[Med] No dependency or build-supply-chain checks.** The Maven wrapper is pinned to Maven 3.8.5 (2022) and has no `distributionSha256Sum`/`wrapperSha256Sum`; dependency versions are fixed by hand (see 6.20) and nothing scans them for known vulnerabilities. **PARTLY DONE, session 43:** PARTLY: `.github/dependabot.yml` added (monthly Maven and Actions updates, at most 5 open pull requests each, once the project is on GitHub). Not done: the Maven wrapper checksums and update (the sandbox cannot reach Maven Central; run `./mvnw wrapper:wrapper -Dmaven=3.9.9` on your machine and commit the result, which adds the `distributionSha256Sum`), the OWASP check in CI, signing the installer.
       Update the wrapper and add the checksums, add the OWASP dependency-check plugin (or GitHub Dependabot alerts) to the CI in 6.1, and sign the Windows installer once one exists, so users are not told to ignore SmartScreen warnings. **(confirmed for the wrapper; the rest is a practice)**
7. [x] **[Low] Anyone at the login screen can lock the admin out for 30 seconds.** `LoginThrottle` counted wrong tries per typed name, so five wrong passwords for `admin` blocked the real admin. **(confirmed by reading)** *(DONE in code, session 44, owner chose to only delay wrong passwords: no more 30-second block. The first 2 wrong passwords are free, then each wrong one makes the next attempt wait 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, then 30 seconds at most; a correct login or 15 quiet minutes resets it. Nobody can be locked out for more than 30 s. The delay is kept in memory only, see 2A.19.)*
8. [x] **[Low] Product pictures are copied without any check.** `ImageStorage.storeNewFile` accepts any file the picker returns, of any size; the extension filter is only a hint in the file dialog. Reject files over about 5 MB, check that the file really is a PNG or JPEG **DONE in code, session 43:** same as 3.12: `ImageStorage.pictureProblem` refuses an empty file, one over 8 MB and a file that does not decode as a picture.
       (first bytes) and read its dimensions before copying, so a huge or damaged picture cannot freeze the screen (extends edge case 3.12 and request 1.2). **(confirmed in code)**
9. [x] **[Low] Security events are not logged.** Failed logins, lockouts, password resets, role changes and account locks leave no log line (`AuthenticationService` has no logger). Log them (never the password) until the audit log in 4.2 exists. **(confirmed)** **DONE in code, session 43:** `SecurityLog` writes failed logins (unknown user, wrong password, locked account, blocked), successful logins, account created / changed / locked / password reset, and the user's own password change, to the log with a SECURITY prefix. Never the password.
10. [x] **[Low] An open session keeps working after its password is reset.** A cashier logged in on another screen carried on after an admin reset or changed that account's password; only a lock or deletion ended the session. **(confirmed)** *(DONE in code, session 46, with no new column and no schema version bump: `Session` remembers the password hash it saw at sign-in; `SessionGuard` (every 30 s and on the next action) compares it with the stored hash and ends the session with the message "Mật khẩu của tài khoản này vừa được đổi hoặc đặt lại." when they differ. A person changing their own password (`PasswordChangeService`) or an admin editing their own account updates the baseline, so they stay signed in. 6 tests in `PasswordResetSessionTest`. Not tried with two real tills.)*
11. [x] **[Low] Passwords stay in memory.** Passwords are held as `String`, and `PBEKeySpec` is never cleared (`spec.clearPassword()`). Little value on a desktop, but a one-line fix in `PasswordUtil`. **(confirmed)** **DONE in code, session 43:** `PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()` and the char array is zeroed after each hash.

12. [x] **[High] The backup button can destroy the database it is meant to protect.** *(Fixed by session 41, see 2.8: the target always ends in `.zip` and is written via a temporary file.)* **NEW (5th scan)** See bug 2.23 (reproduced). Listed here too because it is a data-loss hole reachable by an ordinary admin with one wrong click.
13. [x] **[Low] A stored password hash can make the login hang.** **NEW (5th scan)** `PasswordUtil` reads the iteration count from the stored value and uses it as is. A damaged or hand-edited hash with a huge number (for example `99999999`) keeps a login thread busy for minutes. **DONE in code, session 43:** a stored iteration count outside 1,000 to 5,000,000 is refused as a failed login at once and logged (tested: 99,999,999 and 0 are refused in well under 2 s).
        Anyone who can edit the database can do worse, so this is hygiene: when verifying, reject a count outside a sane range (say 10,000 to 5,000,000) as a failed login and log it. Add a test. **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** a stored count of 20,000,000 made one login take **21 s** (the normal 120,000 takes 0.15 s here), so 99,999,999 would be about 105 s. Counts of 0, -1 and 1 are rejected as a failed login, so my first note about "accepted" was wrong.
14. [ ] **[Low] Logs are readable by every user of the PC and may hold personal data later.** **NEW (5th scan)** At the moment the log lines I searched do not contain phone numbers or names, which is good. Keep it that way (a rule in 6.x: never log customer fields or amounts tied to a person), and when the data folder moves (1.3) set the folder so only the shop's Windows user can read it. **(confirmed for today's lines; the rule is for the future)** *(8th pass: closed on Linux and macOS by the owner-only data folder (the `logs/` folder is inside it). Windows: not done; keep the project folder in a place only the shop's Windows user can open.)*
15. [x] **[Med] Data stays on screen after logout.** **NEW (6th scan)** See bug 2.30: the sales report and the order history (customer names, totals) can remain open on a shared till after the person logged out. **(confirmed by code search)** **DONE in code, session 43:** already fixed in session 42 (2.30): logging out or idle logout closes the history, report and receipt windows.
16. [x] **[Low] Errors are shown with internal wording.** **NEW (6th scan)** Beyond item 5.15: `AlertUtil.error(... e.getMessage())` also shows file paths and SQL-driver text (for example the backup failure I reproduced: `[SQLITE_CANTOPEN] Unable to open the database file (unable to open database: C:\Users\...)`). On a shared till that tells a curious person where the data lives. Show a short Vietnamese message and log the detail. **(reproduced for the backup message)** **DONE in code, session 43:** `AlertUtil.error` now replaces technical text (driver messages, class names, file paths) with a short Vietnamese message and logs the original (`ErrorText`; tested). The start-up failure box still shows the folders on purpose (`errorWithDetails`).

17. [x] **[Med] The bank-transfer QR picture can be swapped by anyone who can write to `data/`, and nothing tells the cashier.** **NEW (7th scan)** The QR is the plain file `data/qr-transfer.png` (item 1.4). Anyone who can write that folder (any Windows user of the PC, or a restore from a USB-stick backup) can replace it with their own account's QR, and every "Chuyển khoản" sale then pays them. The dialog shows the picture with no account holder, bank or "changed on" text, and a change is not logged. Fix: let the admin type the account holder and bank and show them next to the picture; remember the picture's size and hash and warn at start-up (and in the payment dialog) when it changed; set it from inside the app, admin only (feature 4.26), and write the change in the audit log (4.2); restrict the folder to the shop's Windows user. Also check the file size and decode it off the UI thread. **(confirmed in code; the attack itself not tried)** **First step, session 43:** the payment dialog shows the account text from `data/qr-transfer.info` (holder and bank, typed by the owner in a plain text file), remembers the picture's SHA-256 in `qr-transfer.sha256` and shows a red warning, with an admin-only 'xác nhận' button, when the picture is no longer the accepted one; the change is logged (`TransferQrGuard`, tested). The first picture seen is trusted. **DONE in code (session 46) except the folder permission on Windows:** `TransferQrGuard.replace` (admin only) checks the file (PNG/JPEG signature, not empty, at most 2 MB), replaces any older `qr-transfer.*`, saves the account holder and bank (`qr-transfer.info`), records the fingerprint and logs who did it; the admin dashboard has a "Mã QR chuyển khoản" button for it (this is the core of feature 4.26), and opening the admin dashboard checks the picture on a background thread and warns when it differs from the accepted one. The payment dialog already warned (earlier pass). The size/signature check is cheap and avoids decoding the picture; JavaFX still decodes it when shown. 2 new tests. Not done: restricting `data/` to the shop's Windows user (needs `icacls`, 2A.4). The dialogs were compiled but never opened here.
18. [x] **[Med] Passwords reset by an admin are never forced to change.** **NEW (7th scan)** Only the literal default `Admin@123` opened the forced dialog, so a password an admin set or reset was known to the admin and never replaced by the person. **(confirmed in code)** *(DONE in code, session 46: new `users.must_change_password` column (added to both databases through `SqliteSchema.LATER_COLUMNS`, in `MariaDbSchema` and in the importer, so no version bump). It is set when an admin creates an account or sets another person's password, cleared when the person changes it, and `LoginController` shows the forced dialog for it (with its own wording in `PasswordChangeDialog.showForced`). Checked: an existing MariaDB database gets the column on start; an old SQLite file imports. The very first admin created by the first-start screen is not forced. Not tried in the real login screen.)*
19. [ ] **[Low] Restarting the app clears the login delay.** **NEW (7th scan)** `LoginThrottle` keeps its counters in memory only (a `HashMap`; checked in code, 2026-10-17). Closing and reopening the app (about 10 s) resets the growing delay (see 2A.7), so someone at the till can try a few passwords per restart, and different usernames are counted separately. The delay itself is done (session 44); what is **not** done: keep the failure count and the blocked-until time in a small table (a `LATER_COLUMNS`-style schema change on both databases, see 1.5 "A later schema change needs three edits"), or add a short global delay after every wrong password. **(confirmed in code)**
20. [x] **[Low] The password rule is weak.** **NEW (7th scan)** `ValidationUtil.isValidPassword` needs 6 characters with one letter and one digit, so `abc123`, `123456a` and `matkhau1` pass. Ask for 8 or more, refuse the username, the shop name and a short list of common passwords (extends edge case 3.16). **(confirmed)** **DONE in code, session 43:** passwords need 8+ characters with a letter and a digit, not on a short list of common ones (12345678, matkhau1, password123, ...), not containing the shop name; the account dialogs and the change-password dialog also refuse one containing the username. The default `Admin@123` keeps its own message.
21. [x] **[Low] Backup and restore copy files with no size limit, through the system temp folder.** **NEW (7th scan)** `BackupService.extractEntry` copies a zip entry to disk with no cap (a crafted zip can fill the disk), and the temporary database copies (`lunavera-backup*.db`, `lunavera-restore-check*.db`) go to the system temp folder with the customer data and password hashes inside; they are deleted in `finally`, but a crash leaves them. Cap the bytes copied, use `data/tmp/` (cleaned at start-up) instead of the system temp folder. **(confirmed in code)** **First step, session 43:** a restore refuses a file in the zip over 1 GB (database) or 50 MB (a picture), and more than 2 GB of pictures, and removes the half-written file (`BackupService.copyCapped`; tested). **DONE in code (session 46):** temporary copies now go to `data/tmp/` (`DataPaths.createTempFile`), `HelloApplication` empties that folder at start-up (`DataPaths.cleanTmp`), and `BackupService` uses it for its three temporary files. 2 new tests in `DataPathsTest`. The backup/restore flow was not run end to end on Windows.
22. [ ] **[Low] A restore also brings back old accounts and passwords.** **NEW (7th scan)** The `users` table is part of the database, so restoring an older backup re-creates staff who were removed or locked since, and their old passwords (including an old admin password). Pairs with bug 2.41. **(confirmed by reading)** *(PART DONE, session 43: the restore confirmation now says accounts and passwords come back as they were and the accounts list should be checked afterwards.)* Not done: list the locked/removed accounts after the restore for the admin to check.
23. [x] **[Low] Login by phone and the legacy CUSTOMER accounts widen what can be attacked.** **NEW (7th scan)** `UserDAO.findByUsername` also searched `employees.phone` and `customers.phone`, and legacy `CUSTOMER` accounts (from the removed self-registration) could still log in. **(confirmed in code)** *(DONE in code, session 44, owner decision: customers never sign in, only admin and staff. Login by phone number stays for staff. Login no longer looks at customer phones, and an old CUSTOMER account is refused like an unknown name. Old CUSTOMER rows in the database are left alone; locking or removing them would be a numbered migration, not done.)*
24. [x] **[Low] Any signed-in role can change any customer's name and phone, and nothing is logged.** **NEW (7th scan, round 2)** `CustomerController` has no role check and employees can open the customers screen (`openCustomerManagement`). A phone edit moves the loyalty identity to another number. Log the change (audit log 4.2) and consider making the phone change admin-only. **(confirmed in code)** *(DONE in code, session 44, owner decision: changing an existing customer's phone is admin-only: only an admin can change the phone of an existing customer (the field is disabled for others, the save also refuses); an admin's change is written to the security log.)*
- **Session 46 (2 and 2A leftovers):** 2.53, 2A.10, 2A.17, 2A.18 and 2A.21 are done in code and tested (443 tests on SQLite, 415 on MariaDB 10.11). 2A.10 and 2A.18 did not need the `password_changed_at` column or a version bump the 8th-pass note expected: a password-hash baseline and a `must_change_password` column added through `LATER_COLUMNS` were enough. **Still open and honest:** 2A.19 (login delay kept only in memory) and 2A.22 (list locked/removed accounts after a restore); 2A.6 (Maven wrapper checksums, OWASP dependency check) because the sandbox cannot reach Maven Central: run `./mvnw wrapper:wrapper -Dmaven=3.9.9` and `./mvnw org.owasp:dependency-check-maven:check` on a machine with internet; and the two parts of 2A.3/2A.4 that the 8th pass noted as not done (the admin check inside `ProductDAO`/`CategoryDAO` writes, and a passphrase for backups) are still not in the code.
- **Checked in the 7th scan, second scan and fine** (so the next scan need not redo them): no network or process code anywhere (no sockets, `Runtime.exec`, `ProcessBuilder`, `HttpClient`); every SQL statement that takes user input uses `?` parameters (the string-built SQL only joins constant table and column names); restore blocks `../` in picture names; every `onAction` handler and `fx:id` of the 8 FXML screens exists in its controller (mechanical check, 0 missing).




---

25. [ ] **[Med] The security events share one small rotating log, and a flood of failed logins can push the history out.** **NEW (8th scan)** `SecurityLog` writes through `java.util.logging` into the same `data/logs/app-%u-%g.log` as everything else, and `AppLogging` keeps only 3 files of 1 MB. Every wrong password and every attempt made while the delay is running writes a WARNING line (`AuthenticationService.login`), so holding Enter on the login screen fills the 3 MB in a short time and the earlier "discount refused", "account locked", "password reset" lines are overwritten. **(confirmed in code: file count/size and one line per attempt)** Fix: a separate `security.log` handler (bigger, one file per month, nothing else in it), and at most one "blocked" line per username per minute.

26. [ ] **[Med] A MariaDB shop with a second till sends everything over the network unencrypted, and a hung server freezes the till.** **NEW (8th scan)** `DatabaseSettings.jdbcUrl` builds `jdbc:mariadb://host:port/db?connectTimeout=5000` and nothing else: there is no way to ask for TLS (`sslMode`) and no `socketTimeout`. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 allows other tills to connect, and then customer names, phone numbers, password hashes and the database password itself cross the shop's wifi in clear. Without a read timeout, a server that stops answering (power cut at the router, a stuck query) blocks the call forever, and many calls run on the screen thread (see 6.14), so the window freezes with no message. **(confirmed in code)** Fix: optional `db.ssl=true` / `db.options=` in `database.properties`, `socketTimeout` of about 30 s, and say in the docs that a remote server needs TLS.

27. [ ] **[Low] Anyone at the till can keep a known account ("admin") on the login delay.** **NEW (8th scan)** `LoginThrottle` is per user name and the name is the only key, so a customer or a former employee who types "admin" and a wrong password every 30 seconds keeps the real admin waiting up to 30 s at every try; the delay never needs the attacker to succeed. **(reasoned from `LoginThrottle`, not tried)** The delay is short and nobody is locked out, so this is only a nuisance, but it is worth knowing before 2A.19 stores the counters in a table: do not make the delay longer or permanent without an admin way out.

28. [ ] **[Med] The login delay can be skipped by typing the same staff phone number in another spelling.** **NEW (9th scan)** `UserDAO.findByUsername` lets a person sign in with the user name **or** the staff phone, and it normalises the phone first (`+84 901 111 222`, `84901111222`, `0901.111.222` and `0901111222` all find the same account). `LoginThrottle.key` only trims and lower-cases the typed text, and `AuthenticationService.login` asks the throttle with the text as typed. So one account has one throttle bucket for its user name and a separate fresh bucket (2 free tries, then the delay) for every spelling of its phone: dozens of buckets, and the 30-second ceiling never bites. A staff phone number is much easier to learn than a password. **(confirmed in code; not tried against a running app)** Fix: throttle on the account that was found (its id) as well as on the typed text, or normalise the text with `ValidationUtil.normalizePhone` before it becomes the key; add a test that five spellings of one phone share one counter.

29. [ ] **[Low] A restore accepts a backup that has no active administrator.** **NEW (9th scan)** `BackupService.verifyDatabase` checks `integrity_check`, the schema version and that the tables `users`, `products` and `orders` exist, nothing about the accounts. A backup made before the "last administrator" rule (2A.2) existed, or one whose admins were all locked, restores without error; after the restart nobody can sign in as admin, and the first-start dialog does not help because accounts exist. **(confirmed what is checked; the situation is rare)** Fix: in `verifyDatabase` also require `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users WHERE role='ADMIN' AND status='ACTIVE'` to be at least 1, and say so in the restore dialog (5.22 already shows what a backup holds).

30. [ ] **[Low] Looking at a backup before restoring it reads the zip without the size limits the restore uses.** **NEW (10th scan)** `BackupService.inspectInto` copies the `lunavera.db` entry with a plain `Files.copy(zip, databaseCopy, ...)` and reads the info entry with `zip.readAllBytes()`, while the restore itself (item 2A.21) uses `copyCapped(..., MAX_DATABASE_BYTES)` and `copyCapped(..., MAX_PICTURE_BYTES)`. So a damaged or deliberately made zip (or the wrong big file picked by mistake) can fill the temporary disk or the memory during the preview, before any limit applies. **(confirmed in code; not tried with a real oversized zip)** Use `copyCapped` for the database entry, cap the info entry at a few KB, and add a test with an entry larger than the cap (the cap can be a parameter so the test stays small).

31. [ ] **[Low] Typing 1,000 different user names wipes the login delay of every account, "admin" included.** **NEW (10th scan)** `LoginThrottle.recordFailure` does `states.clear()` when `MAX_TRACKED` (1000) names are tracked, and the comment says an attacker "only resets everyone's counters". Each wrong attempt costs the attacker one password hash (a fraction of a second), so a script or a keyboard-emulating USB stick needs only minutes to reset the delay and then get two free tries again. It is a speed bump rather than a lock, so this is Low, but the cap that protects memory should not also erase the protection. **(confirmed in code)** When the map is full, drop the expired entries first, then the oldest ones, never all; test with 1,001 names that the entry for "admin" survives. Related: 2A.19, 2A.27, 2A.28.

32. [ ] **[Low] The first login with a name that does not exist is slower than the next ones.** **NEW (10th scan)** `AuthenticationService.dummyHash()` builds its throwaway hash on first use (`PasswordUtil.hash(...)`), so that first attempt runs the slow password function twice and every later one only once; a stopwatch can tell "no such user" from "wrong password" for that one try. The dummy hash also has the current 600,000 iterations, while a stored hash that has not been upgraded yet (2.12) has the old count, so those accounts take a different time. **(confirmed in code; the difference is real but small, and the message is already the same)** Build the dummy hash once at start-up in the background, and compare against a hash with the same iteration count as the one stored.

33. [ ] **[Low] A test switch inside the shipped program can create the well-known administrator.** **NEW (10th scan)** `DatabaseConfig.initialize()` still contains the old seeding code: with `-Dlunavera.seedDefaultAdmin=true` and no account in the database it inserts `admin` with `Admin@123` (`SEED_DEFAULT_ADMIN_PROPERTY`, lines 418 and 493-520). The forced password change (2.2) still stops that account from being used for long, and a person who can start the program with extra options can do much more anyway, so this is a hygiene item: test-only behaviour and the default-password constant should not ship. **(confirmed in code; the property is set only in `TestDatabaseSupport`)** Move the seeding into `TestDatabaseSupport` (it can insert the user itself with `PasswordUtil.hash`), keep only the constant that the "refuse the default password" checks need, and delete the property.

34. [ ] **[Med] The transfer-QR integrity record lives in the same folder as the picture, so whoever can swap the picture can also reset the check.** **NEW (11th scan)** `TransferQrGuard.inspect` compares the picture with `qr-transfer.sha256` next to it; if that file is missing it records the current picture and returns `FIRST_SEEN`, which is trusted silently (the only callers treat everything except `CHANGED` as fine), and any `IOException` also returns `OK`. So the attack that 2A.17 was made to stop (put your own account's QR in `data/`) only needs one more step: delete `qr-transfer.sha256` (or write the new picture's hash into it), and the next start accepts the new picture without a warning, a log line apart. The account text file `qr-transfer.info` has the same weakness. **(confirmed in code; not tried)** Keep the accepted fingerprint (and the holder/bank text) in the database (the `app_flags` style table, or a small `settings` table from 4.7) where only the app's login can change it, treat "picture present but no record" as `CHANGED` for an employee till (an admin accepts it once from the dashboard button that already exists), and treat a read error as `CHANGED`, not `OK`.

35. [ ] **[Low] `AccountService` does not enforce the username, password and phone rules; only the dialogs do.** **NEW (11th scan)** `ValidationUtil.isValidUsername` and `isValidPassword` are called from `AccountController`'s dialog and `FirstRunDialog` only; `AccountService.create`, `createFirstAdmin` and `update` store whatever they are given (a 1-character user name, an empty-looking password that is not blank, a password that is on the common list). Today only those two dialogs call the service, so nothing is exposed, but the service is the place item 6.21 wants the rules in, and a second caller (a CSV import of staff, 4.12) would skip them. There is also no upper limit on a password: `isValidPassword` has only a minimum, so a pasted 1 MB text makes PBKDF2 copy it for each of the 600,000 rounds. **(confirmed in code)** Check username, password (add a maximum such as 128 characters) and phone inside `AccountService` and let the dialogs only show the message; add the cases to `AccountServiceTest`.

36. [ ] **[Low] The database guide gives the app's user every privilege on its database, for ever.** **NEW (11th scan)** `docs/DATABASE.md` section 2 uses `GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON lunavera.* TO 'lunavera'@'localhost'` (and the same for the other tills' network address). The app needs `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` every day and `CREATE, ALTER, INDEX, TRIGGER, REFERENCES` only when it starts and creates or upgrades tables; it never needs `DROP`, `GRANT OPTION`, `CREATE USER` or access to other databases. With ALL, a stolen till or a leaked `database.properties` can drop every table. **(confirmed in the guide)** Document the narrower grant (`SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, CREATE, ALTER, INDEX, REFERENCES, TRIGGER, LOCK TABLES`), say that `DROP` stays with the owner's own account in phpMyAdmin, and check on a real server that the app still starts and migrates with it.

37. [ ] **[Low] The login delay and the idle logout both read the PC's clock, so changing the time defeats or triggers them.** **NEW (11th scan)** `LoginThrottle` uses `System.currentTimeMillis()` for `blockedUntil`, and `IdleTracker` uses `Instant.now()`. Anyone at the till who sets the Windows clock forward a minute clears every login delay (1.5 minutes of guesses saved per try), and a clock moved back by an hour turns a 30-second delay into a one-hour block for that name; `IdleTracker` never logs out while the clock is behind and logs out at once when it jumps ahead. (`ClockCheck` only looks at start-up.) **(confirmed in code)** Measure both with `System.nanoTime()` (it does not follow clock changes); the tests already inject a clock/instant, so only the default supplier changes.

38. [ ] **[Low] The error filter is a deny-list, so many raw English and driver texts still reach the screen, one of them with the database user and host.** **NEW (16th scan)** `ErrorText.forScreen` hides a message only if it matches `TECHNICAL` (starts with Failed/Unable/Cannot/Could not/Error, mentions `SQLException`, a Java package, a drive path or a Unix folder). Run over 30 typical messages **(reproduced)**: these are *shown to the cashier as they are*: "No space left on device", "Access is denied", "Permission denied", "database is locked", "Connection refused: connect", "Communications link failure", "Table 'lunavera.orders' doesn't exist", "Duplicate entry 'admin' for key 'username'", "Data too long for column 'name' at row 1", "Unknown column 'cost' in 'field list'", "Unknown database 'lunavera'", "The process cannot access the file because it is being used by another process" (without a path) and **"Access denied for user 'lunavera'@'localhost' (using password: YES)"**, which prints the database user and host in a dialog. A null or blank message is passed through as null or blank (an empty dialog). Most of these come from `IOException` and `SQLException` messages shown through `AlertUtil.error(..., e.getMessage())`. **(confirmed by running the class)** Turn it round into an allow-list: show a message only when it is the app's own (a small `UserMessage` exception type, or text that contains Vietnamese letters or the known sentences of `DataAccessException.hintFor`), log everything else and show `GENERIC` (or the more specific hint of 2.78 and 5.30, which need the cause, not the text). A blank message becomes `GENERIC`. Tests with the 30 texts above. Related: 2A.16, 5.15, 6.10.
39. [ ] **[Med] A password typed into the user-name box is written to the log in clear text.** **NEW (18th scan)** `AuthenticationService.login` logs the typed name when no such account exists (`failed login: no such user '<typed text>'`) and in the "login blocked" line; `SecurityLog.clean` only strips line breaks and tabs. A cashier who types the password in the user-name box (or swaps the two boxes) puts the password into `data/logs/` and into the console, readable by anyone who can open that folder or a copied log. **Reproduced:** `login("Quan-Cafe#2026", "nguyenvan")` logs `WARNING: SECURITY: failed login: no such user 'Quan-Cafe#2026'`. The README rule "it never records passwords" (about `SecurityLog`) is therefore not true for this case. Fix: for an unknown name log only its length and the first two characters (`'Qu…' (14 characters)`), or the user id when it exists and nothing when it does not; apply the same to the blocked and locked lines (a locked account is fine, its name is a real one). Add a test that a name that is not an account never appears in full in the log. Related: 2A.9, 6.28.

40. [ ] **[Low] The restore preview has no size limit, so a damaged or crafted backup can fill the disk before the restore's own limits apply.** **NEW (24th scan)** `BackupService.inspectInto` (used by the dialog's preview and by `scheduleRestore`) copies the zip entry `lunavera.db` into a temp file with a plain `Files.copy` and reads every other entry to its end; the 1 GiB, 50 MiB and 2 GiB caps (`MAX_DATABASE_BYTES`, `MAX_PICTURE_BYTES`, `MAX_PICTURES_TOTAL_BYTES`) are used only later, in the step that applies the restore (`copyCapped`). A zip whose `lunavera.db` expands to tens of gigabytes (a small file of repeated bytes) fills the disk under `data/tmp` while the person only wanted to *look* at it, and the till then fails every sale with a disk-full error (2.78) until the temp file is removed at the next start. **(confirmed in code; not tried with a bomb)** Use `copyCapped` for the database entry and count the bytes of the other entries against the same caps inside `inspectInto`; test with a generated zip that expands past the limit.

41. [ ] **[Low] `SecurityLog.clean` removes only carriage return, line feed and tab, so other line separators and control characters can still forge or hide log lines.** **NEW (24th scan)** The cleaner replaces `[\r\n\t]` and cuts at 40 characters. Unicode line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029, U+0085), the vertical tab and form feed (U+000B, U+000C) and the ESC character (U+001B) pass through; some log viewers and editors start a new line at them, and a terminal that shows the log (`tail`) acts on ESC sequences. Anyone can type them into the user-name box (2A.39 is the same box). **(confirmed in code; a viewer that splits on U+2028 is needed to see the effect)** Replace every character that is an ISO control character or in the Unicode categories `Zl`/`Zp` with a space, keep the 40-character cut, and add the cases to a `SecurityLogTest`.

## 3. Niche and edge cases (most important first)
1. [ ] **Stock is only taken at payment.** Two open tables can both hold the last item and the second payment fails at the end; the product list on screen is
       also loaded once and can be out of date. Consider reserving stock when a line is added, or re-checking stock before the payment dialog. **(confirmed)** *(8th pass: not done: needs a design decision (reserve at add, or re-check before the payment dialog) and changes the POS screen, which cannot be loaded here.)*
2. [x] **Editing a product can overwrite sales.** **NEW (2nd scan)** `ProductDAO.update` writes `stock = ?` with the number that was in the edit dialog. If a sale (another window, or a payment while the dialog is open)
       changes the stock after the dialog opened, saving the dialog puts the old number back and the sold items reappear. Fix: save stock changes as a difference
       (`stock = stock + ?`) or compare with the stock read when the dialog opened, and warn on a mismatch. **(confirmed)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** stock 10, a sale of 3 (stock 7), then saving a product object loaded before the sale (only the name changed) put the stock back to **10**. **DONE in code, 8th pass:** `Product` remembers the stock it was read with (`loadedStock`); `ProductDAO.update` writes `stock=COALESCE(?, stock)` and sends NULL when the stock number was not changed, so a rename or price edit no longer puts the old stock back over sales made while the dialog was open (the 6th-scan case: 10, sale of 3, rename: now stays 7). A stock number that was typed in is still written as typed (a stock-take); a sale made while the dialog was open is then not added back, which is what typing a counted number means. A product object that was not read from the database (new, or built in code) is still written in full. Checked: the new SQL on real SQLite (rename-only keeps 7, typed 20 gives 20, typed 0 gives 0); the changed files compile with the JDK compiler (0 errors); two tests added in `ProductDAOTest` (compile-checked, **not run**: no database driver here, so they need your `mvn test`).
3. [x] **Two copies of the app on the same database.** There is no single-instance lock, so a double-click opens a second window on the same SQLite file. **(confirmed)** **DONE in code, session 43:** two copies on one data folder: the first holds a lock on `lunavera.lock`; the second shows 'đang chạy' and exits (`SingleInstance`; checked with two real processes, and the lock frees itself when the first ends).
4. [ ] **Customers can never be deleted or merged.** **NEW (2nd scan)** By design (past orders point to them), but a duplicate or a customer who asks to be removed cannot be handled in the app. See feature 4.8. **(confirmed)** *(8th pass: not done: this is feature 4.8, a screen plus merge rules.)*
5. [x] **A sale that crosses midnight** shows under different days in the order history (created date) and the report/dashboard (paid date). Decide one rule and say so on screen. **(confirmed)** **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner decided: the PAY date counts for money. One rule in `OrderDAO.EFFECTIVE_DAY` (paid day, else opened day for orders never paid), used by the report summary, the dashboard counts and the history date filter; tested with an order opened 23:50 and paid 00:10. *(8th pass: the rule is now applied in every report and dashboard query; before, the best-seller, daily, profit and "today revenue" queries still used `paid_at` alone. See 3.23.)*
6. [x] **Only Vietnamese phone numbers are accepted** (`0…`, `84…`, `+84…`); a foreign customer's number is rejected. **(confirmed)** **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner decided (session 43): NO foreign numbers (they are usually one-off visitors; a walk-in needs no phone). Nothing to change.
7. [x] **Cancelling a paid order after points were spent** takes points down to a floor of 0, so a customer can keep more than they earned. **(confirmed)** **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner decided (session 43): keep the current behaviour (points never go below 0). Accepted as it is.
8. [x] **Any employee can empty a table and so cancel another cashier's unpaid order.** **NEW (2nd scan)** There is a confirm dialog but I found no role check in `OrderController.handleToggleTableStatus`, and nothing records who did it **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner decided: only the cashier who opened the order, or an admin, may empty a table that has an unpaid order (`TablePolicy`); others get a message. An order with no cashier recorded is admin-only. Still not recorded: an audit log of who did it (feature 4.2).
       (see feature 4.2, audit log). **(confirmed for the code path; intended policy is the owner's call)**
9. [x] **Customers who give a name but no phone** get no loyalty points and cannot be found later. By design, but staff may not know. Say so near the phone field. **(confirmed)** **DONE in code, session 43:** typing a name without a phone shows a line under the phone box saying no points are given.
10. [x] **Order times use the computer's local clock.** A wrong system date puts orders on the wrong day. **(inferred)** **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner asked for a warning: at start-up, if the computer's clock is more than 10 minutes behind the newest saved order, a message says the date may be wrong (`ClockCheck`).
11. [x] **Product price changed while an order is open:** lines already in the cart keep the old price. Probably right; decide it on purpose. **(confirmed)** **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner decided (session 43): leave as it is (lines already in the cart keep the old price).
12. [x] **Product pictures are not checked** for size or real image content, only the file extension filter. **(inferred)** **DONE in code, session 43:** `ImageStorage.pictureProblem` refuses an empty file, one over 8 MB and a file that does not decode as a picture; checked with a real PNG and a real .jfif in a JavaFX run.
13. [ ] **[Med] Cancelling a paid order changes an earlier day's totals.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `ReportDAO` counts revenue by the `paid_at` day and only for status `PAID`. A sale cancelled today but paid last week disappears from last week's
        revenue, after that week's cash was already counted, and no refund date is stored, so today shows nothing. Decide the rule (refunds count on the day they were made is the usual one) and store `cancelled_at` (see feature 4.2). **(confirmed by reading the queries)** *(8th pass: not done: needs a `cancelled_at` column in both table definitions, the importer, a migration and report changes.)*
14. [x] **[Low] The session check pauses while another window is open.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `SessionGuard.tick` returns early when any window other than the main one is showing, so a locked account stays logged in for as long as an **DONE in code, session 43:** the 30-second check now also runs while the history, report or receipt window is open; only a modal dialog (payment, form) postpones a forced logout, and only for 2 checks (`SessionGuard.shouldPostpone`, tested). Not run end-to-end: a real lock of an account while those windows are open (the timer is 30 s).
        order-history or report window stays open. It is still re-checked on screen switches, payments and admin actions, so this is a short gap, not a hole. **(confirmed)**
15. [x] **[Low] The phone pattern accepts 11 digits.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `ValidationUtil` allows `0` or `84` followed by 9 or 10 digits, so `0` plus 10 digits passes. Current Vietnamese mobile numbers are `0` plus 9 digits; the 10-digit tail only fits old landlines. A typo with one extra digit is accepted and becomes a new customer. **DONE / DECIDED, session 43:** owner decided: only 10-digit mobile numbers (`0` + 9 digits, or +84 / 84 + 9 digits). An older 11-digit number already stored is not changed, but cannot be saved again as typed until it is corrected.
        Decide whether landlines count. **(confirmed)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `isValidPhone("01234567890")` = true.
16. [x] **[Low] A password may equal the username.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `isValidPassword` only needs 6 characters with a letter and a digit, so `admin123` for the user `admin123` is accepted. Reject a password that contains the username, and the default `Admin@123` **DONE in code, session 43:** a password that contains the username is refused when creating or editing an account and when changing a password (`ValidationUtil.containsUsername`). The default `Admin@123` was already refused.
        (already rejected in the first-login prompt, but not when an admin resets another account). **(confirmed)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `isValidPassword("admin123")` and `isValidPassword("Admin@123")` are both true.
17. [ ] **[Low] Cash received and change are never stored.** **NEW (3rd scan)** The payment dialog works out the change, but the order keeps only method, discount and total, so a shift's cash cannot be reconciled afterwards (needed by feature 4.6). **(confirmed)** *(8th pass: not done: needs new order columns in both table definitions, a migration and the payment dialog.)*

18. [ ] **[Med] Vietnamese text can be stored two ways, and uniqueness is case-sensitive.** **NEW (4th scan)** There was no Unicode normalisation: the same word typed with composed or separate accent marks ("Trà") looks identical but is different text, and `Trà` and `trà` were three different categories **(reproduced in the 6th scan)**. *(PART DONE in code, session 43: names (category, product, customer, employee) are stored in the composed form (`TextNormalizer.nfc`) and compared with `TextNormalizer.key` (no case, no extra spaces, either accent encoding); a category with the same name is refused, and the POS and customer searches normalise what is typed. Usernames also have a case-insensitive unique index on SQLite (`ux_users_username_nocase`). Names saved before this stay as they were until they are edited and saved.)* **Not done:** a database-level unique rule for category names (checked 2026-10-17: only the app refuses duplicates), and on MariaDB the username index (see 1.9).
19. [x] **[Low] A product can be saved with a negative cost, or a cost above its price.** **NEW (4th scan)** The product dialog checks that price is above 0 and stock is not negative, but not the cost (`AdminController`, around line 1302). Reject a negative cost and warn when cost is higher than price. **(confirmed)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan at the DAO level:** a product with cost -5000 and price 1000 was saved; the dialog's own check was not run (it needs the screen). **DONE in code, session 43:** a negative cost is refused and a cost above the price asks 'vẫn lưu?'.
20. [x] **[Low] Esc throws away a half-filled form.** **NEW (4th scan)** Since Esc was bound to Cancel in every dialog, pressing it by accident in the product or account form discards everything typed, with no question. Ask only when something was changed. **(inferred from the key binding)** **DONE in code, session 43:** Esc, Cancel and the window's X ask 'Bỏ thay đổi?' (default Không) when something in the product, category, account or customer form changed; nothing changed closes at once (`AlertUtil.confirmBeforeDiscarding`; the four answers were tried in a real dialog).
21. [ ] **[Low] One low-stock number for every product.** **NEW (4th scan)** `ProductDAO.LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD` is 10 for everything: too low for a fast seller, too high for a slow one. See feature 4.19. **(confirmed)** *(8th pass: not done: needs a new product column, a migration and the product dialog.)*

22. [x] **[Med] Two OPEN orders on one table are not prevented, and the table map then picks one at random.** **NEW (5th scan)** With two open orders on the same table the table map and the order that opens could disagree **(reproduced in the 6th scan)**. *(DONE, 13th pass: `OrderDAO.insert` refuses a second OPEN order for a table (`rejectIfTableHasOpenOrder`, message "Bàn N đang có đơn chưa thanh toán (#id)..."), and `TableDAO.findAll` takes `MAX(id)`, so the table map shows the newest open order, never a random one. On SQLite the database itself also refuses it: a partial unique index `ux_orders_one_open_per_table` (`SchemaMigrations.ensureOneOpenOrderPerTable`, built at every start; skipped with a warning while old duplicates exist, like the phone index), and `OrderDAO.insert` turns that refusal (two tills at the same moment) into the same clear message. So `nguyễn`, `NGUYỄN` and `nguyen` all find `Nguyễn`, and `dao` finds `Đào`. New `VietnameseSearchTest` (5; the first four also run on MariaDB). The folding rule was checked in Python. Fix in the code: add `AND EFFECTIVE_DAY <= ?` with today (and ignore a result row outside the 7 keys); the test then passes in any order. Also make the dashboard test not depend on what other tests left in the shared database (or give `EdgeCasesTest` its own data folder). **(reproduced with a stand-in runner; not seen under Maven, whose order may hide it)**

[ ] **[Low] A product picture is limited by file size only, not by its width and height, and it is decoded in full on the screen thread.** **NEW (8th scan)** `ImageStorage.pictureProblem` refuses files over 8 MB, then opens the whole picture with `new Image(uri, false)` to see that it decodes. A phone photo of 12,000 x 9,000 pixels or a flat-colour PNG of 20,000 x 20,000 pixels is under 8 MB yet needs hundreds of MB to a few GB when decoded, so picking it can freeze the dialog or end in an `OutOfMemoryError`. `ProductController` (`AdminController` before the split) calls it from the file-picker handler, i.e. on the screen thread. **(confirmed in code that no pixel limit exists; the effect is reasoned)** Fix: read the width and height from the first bytes (PNG header, JPEG start-of-frame) like `TransferQrGuard` does for the QR picture, refuse above about 4,000 x 4,000, and run the check in `Async`.

44. [ ] **[Low] A data folder inside OneDrive, Dropbox or Google Drive is not noticed.** **NEW (8th scan)** The data folder is `<start-up folder>/data` (`DataPaths.dataDir`). On Windows the Desktop and Documents folders are often redirected into OneDrive, and a sync client then copies, locks or "conflict-renames" `lunavera.db`, its `-wal`/`-shm` files and the backup zips while the app is running; WAL even falls back silently on such folders (`DatabaseConfig.enableWriteAheadLog`). Nothing in `src` looks for these folder names (searched). Fix: at start-up, if the path contains `OneDrive`, `Dropbox` or `Google Drive`, show the same kind of one-time warning as the other start-up checks and write it in the README ("keep the app and `data/` in `C:\\Lunavera`, and copy backups into the cloud folder instead").

[ ] **[Low] Five status parsers and a few searches change letter case without a locale.** **NEW (8th scan)** `PaymentMethod.fromDb`, `Role.fromDb`, `TableStatus.fromDb`, `OrderStatus.fromDb`, `AccountStatus.fromDb` call `value.trim().toUpperCase()`, and `MoneyParser`, `BackupService`, `CustomerController`, `AccountController`, `ProductController` (all in `AdminController` before the split) call `toLowerCase()`, all with the computer's default locale. On a PC set to Turkish or Azerbaijani the letter "i" becomes a dotted capital "İ", so "paid" turns into "PAİD" and "admin" into "ADMİN", `valueOf` throws, and the screen that reads that value fails to load. `LoginThrottle` and `ImageStorage` already use `Locale.ROOT`. **(confirmed by grep)** Fix: `Locale.ROOT` everywhere, and a test that sets `Locale.setDefault(new Locale("tr"))`.

46. [ ] **[Low] A stock typed as "1.5" in a CSV file becomes 15.** **NEW (9th scan)** `ProductCsv.parse` reads the stock with `Integer.parseInt(s.replace(".", "").replace(" ", ""))`, i.e. every dot is a thousands separator. A cell saved by Excel as `1.5`, `2.50` or `0.5` is therefore read as 15, 250 or 5 without any message (the limit of 1,000,000 is the only check). **(confirmed in code)** Fix: accept a dot as a separator only when it sits in a group of exactly three digits (`1.000`, `12.500`); anything else with a dot or comma is the problem line "tồn kho phải là số nguyên".

47. [ ] **[Low] The apostrophe that protects a CSV cell from Excel formulas is shown by Excel when it opens a CSV file.** **NEW (9th scan)** `Csv.cell` puts `'` in front of a value that starts with `= + - @` (tab, carriage return) and its comment says Excel hides it. That is true for a cell typed into Excel, not for a CSV file opened in it: the apostrophe stays visible, so the accountant sees `'-5% combo` or `'+84...` in the orders and customers exports. Only our own product import knows to take it off again (`ProductCsv.unprotect`). **(reasoned from how Excel opens CSV files; not checked with Excel)** Fix: protect only what can really run, i.e. values starting with `=` or `@`, and `+`/`-` when the next character is not a digit, space or `.`; keep the apostrophe for those, and add a test with `-5% combo`, `+84901234567` and `=SUM(A1)`.

A table 13 (once 4.18 lets the owner add tables), or a gap (table 5 removed), puts buttons in the wrong cells or leaves holes, and the grid has no scroll. **(confirmed in code; cannot happen today because tables cannot be added)** Build the grid from the sorted list of tables (a `TilePane` or `FlowPane` fills itself), do it when 4.18 is built, and cover it with a test of the position function.

49. [ ] **[Med] The exported report CSV probably opens in a single column on a Windows set to the Vietnamese number format.** **NEW (10th scan)** `ReportWindow` writes comma-separated lines with a BOM (`\uFEFF`) and `Csv.cell` quotes on commas; there is no `sep=` line. Excel splits a CSV at the Windows *list separator*, and where the decimal sign is a comma (the Vietnamese format) that separator is normally a semicolon, so the whole row lands in column A. The import side already knows this (`ProductCsv.split` picks `;` when the first line has more of them), the export does not. **(reasoned from how Excel reads CSV files; not tried, there is no Excel here)** Write `sep=,` as the first line (Excel honours it in every regional setting; other programs show it as one odd line, which 3.47 should take into account), or let the export button offer "dấu phẩy" and "dấu chấm phẩy". Check on the shop's own Excel first. Related: 3.47, 4.21.
