[ ] **[Low] Two tills that start together after an upgrade both run the same MariaDB migration step.** **NEW (11th scan)** `MariaDbMigrations.upgrade` reads the schema version, runs each missing step and writes the new version, with no lock. `STEPS` is empty today, so nothing happens yet, but the first real step (an `ALTER TABLE`) run by two tills in the same second makes one fail with "duplicate column" or run twice, and that till shows "cannot start". The same goes for the table creation in `initialize()` (the `IF NOT EXISTS` form is safe, the data-rule `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT` check-then-add is not, but its failure is only logged). **(reasoned from the code; not tried, there is no second till)** Take `SELECT GET_LOCK('lunavera-migrate', 30)` around the version check and the steps, release it in `finally`, and re-read the version after getting the lock.

51. [ ] **[Low] On a MariaDB shop every till makes its own hourly full copy of the database, and the 10 copies cover only 10 hours.** **NEW (11th scan)** `SqliteSnapshotService.start()` runs on every till whose database is not SQLite, so with 3 tills the server is read in full three times an hour and each till keeps its own `data/snapshots/`. Ten snapshots are kept, so a mistake that is noticed the next morning (a wrong bulk import, a deleted product list) is already in all of them; only the daily `mysqldump` zips (14 kept) reach further back. **(confirmed in code)** Run it on one till only (a `db.snapshots=true` setting, default on for the first till), or keep a thinned set (the last 6 hours, then one per day for a week); document it in `docs/DATABASE.md` next to the backup text.

52. [ ] **[Low] "Gắn khách hàng" loads every customer into a drop-down list.** **NEW (11th scan)** `OrderController.handleEditOrder` calls `customerDAO.findAll()` on the screen thread and puts all rows in a `ChoiceDialog`. A shop with a few thousand customers gets a freeze and a list nobody can scroll; the phone box (which already looks a customer up) is the normal way. **(confirmed in code)** Replace the dialog with a search box that uses the existing history/customer search, limited to 20 rows, and run it through `Async.run` (6.14).

53. [ ] **[Med] The profit figures of the report are wrong for discounted orders, and old days change when a cost is edited.** **NEW (12th scan)** Item 4.20 records both limits inside a ticked note but nothing is open. `ReportDAO.profit`, `topProducts` and `dailySales` compute `line_total - quantity * products.cost` per line: (1) the order's discount is not subtracted, while "revenue" is the order total after the discount, so for a discounted order the report says revenue minus costs is less than the profit it prints; with a 20 % staff discount on a shop's busy day the "Lợi nhuận gộp" can be well above the truth; (2) the cost is read from the product row *now*, so raising a cost today lowers the profit of last month, and lines of a product whose cost was empty then and filled in later start to count. **(confirmed in code)** Store the cost on the order line when the order is paid (`order_items.unit_cost`, a `LATER_COLUMNS` entry like 2A.18's, in both schemas and the importer, copied from `products.cost` in `OrderDAO`), compute profit from it, and spread the order discount over its lines in proportion to `line_total` (or subtract the order's discount once per order). Keep "-" for lines with no cost known at the time. Tests: a discounted order whose revenue minus cost equals the printed profit; a cost edit that does not move an earlier day.

54. [ ] **[Low] The order CSV export stops at 200,000 rows without saying so.** **NEW (12th scan)** `OrderDAO.findAllForExport` calls `findHistoryPage(filter, sort, EXPORT_LIMIT, 0)` with `EXPORT_LIMIT = 200_000`, and the history window answers "Đã lưu N đơn" with N the number of rows read, so a shop with more matching orders gets a file that silently ends early, and all 200,000 `OrderSummary` objects are held in memory before the file is written. **(confirmed in code; a shop reaches 200,000 orders only after years)** Read one more row than the limit and say "Chỉ 200.000 đơn đầu tiên được xuất; hãy chọn khoảng ngày nhỏ hơn" when it is there; better, write the lines while reading (a cursor), so there is no limit and no big list. Test with the limit lowered through a parameter.

55. [ ] **[Low] The same names are different on SQLite but the same on MariaDB.** **NEW (13th scan)** `MariaDbSchema` builds every table with `COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, which ignores case **and accents**, and `categories.name` is `UNIQUE` (line 63); on SQLite the same column is a plain `UNIQUE` text (`SqliteSchema` line 56). On MariaDB the categories "Trà" and "Tra" (or "Cà phê" and "Ca phe") are one name, on SQLite they are two, and the product-name check in the app (`existsByNameInCategory`) answers differently on the two databases. Copying a SQLite shop into MariaDB (`SqliteToMariaDbImporter`) can therefore stop on a duplicate key for data that was valid before. **(confirmed from the two schema files; a failing import was not tried)** Decide one rule (accent-insensitive on both is the friendlier one, see 3.18 and 1.9), make the SQLite side follow it with the `VN_KEY` function, and let the importer report the two clashing names instead of failing half-way.
56. [ ] **[Low] The history search cannot find the transfer reference the customer typed.** **NEW (15th scan)** The payment dialog tells a customer paying by transfer to write `DH000012` (`OrderController` builds it with `String.format("%06d", id)`), so that is what the shop's bank statement shows. The history search only understands digits without a leading zero and `#12`, and the screens show the order as `#12` (5.12). **Reproduced on the real database code:** `DH000012`, `000012` and `# 12` all return 0 rows for an existing order. The accountant must convert by hand. Put the reference in one helper next to `OrderNumber` (`transferNote(id)` and its reverse) and let the history search accept `DH000012` and `#12` alike. Related: 4.31.
57. [ ] **[Low] A password with accented letters can stop working when it is typed on another keyboard.** **NEW (15th scan)** `PasswordUtil.hash/matches` use the characters as typed, and Vietnamese letters exist in a composed and a decomposed Unicode form that look the same. **Reproduced:** `hash("mậtkhẩu1")` does not match the decomposed spelling of the same word, and `ValidationUtil.isValidPassword` accepts accented letters, so a password set on one device may be refused on another (phone keyboards, macOS, pasted text). Normalise to NFC (`java.text.Normalizer`) in `hash` and `matches` (hashes of plain ASCII passwords stay valid), or say in the password rule that letters without accents are required. Related: 3.18.
58. [ ] **[Low] The order history accepts a "from" date after the "to" date and just shows nothing.** **NEW (15th scan)** `ReportWindow` refuses that range with a message; `OrderHistoryWindow` builds its `HistoryFilter` from the two pickers without any check (no `isAfter` in the class), so the list is empty with no explanation, and the admin's "Xuất CSV" ("every order matching the filters", 4.21) writes an empty file. **(confirmed in code)** Show the same warning, or swap the two dates.

59. [ ] **[Low] There is no sensible upper limit on a price or a quantity, and the loyalty points wrap round to a negative number above 21 trillion dong.** **NEW (16th scan)** The only limit on a price is the 15 digits of `MoneyParser` (999 trillion dong), a quantity has none (`Cart.setQuantity` takes any `int`; 5.27 will add a typed box), and `LoyaltyPolicy.pointsFor` ends with `.intValue()`. **Reproduced by running the classes:** an order total of 25,000,000,000,000 gives **-1,794,967,296** points, 21,474,836,480,000 gives -2,147,483,648, and `Cart.totalQuantity()` of two lines of 2,000,000,000 gives -294,967,296. A price of 999,999,999,999,999 times 9,000 is stopped cleanly by `Money.toDong` ("Số tiền quá lớn hoặc không hợp lệ"), so only the points wrap silently, but a price typed with three extra zeros (an easy slip, 2.77 shows others) is saved without any question and every later sale of it is odd. **(confirmed by running the code)** Ask the owner for a ceiling (for example 100,000,000đ for a price and 999 for a quantity of one line), check it in the product form, `ProductService` and the CSV import, `Cart.setQuantity` and the typed box of 5.27; make `pointsFor` clamp with `Math.toIntExact` guarded by a `min`; make `totalQuantity` a `long` or stop at the cap. Tests with the numbers above.
60. [ ] **[Low] A fractional amount is shown one way and stored another, and a tiny negative one shows as "-0đ".** **NEW (17th scan)** `CurrencyUtil.format` uses a `DecimalFormat` (half-even rounding) while `Money.toDong` rounds half-up. **Reproduced:** `0.5` is shown as `0đ` but stored as 1; `-0.0000001` is shown as `-0đ`. No screen produces a fraction today (every amount is typed or computed in whole dong), so this only bites when one does, for example after 2.77 is fixed to allow decimals, a percentage discount (4.11) or an import of an old REAL column. Make both use the same `RoundingMode.HALF_UP`, and format a value that rounds to 0 as `0đ`. Add the two cases to `CurrencyUtilTest`.

61. [ ] **[Low] A tab-separated product file (Excel "Text (Tab delimited)" or "Unicode Text") is refused with a misleading message.** **NEW (19th scan)** `ProductCsv.parse` accepts commas and semicolons (and a byte-order mark, CRLF, a missing last line break and blank lines: all tried and fine), but a file saved with tabs gives "Dòng 1: thiếu cột bắt buộc. Cần có các cột ..." although the three columns are there. **(reproduced by running `ProductCsv.parse`)** Fix: also try the tab as a separator when the header has no comma or semicolon, or say "tệp phân cách bằng dấu tab: hãy lưu lại dạng CSV UTF-8".

62. [ ] **[Low] The SQLite-to-MariaDB import opens the old file read-write and never looks at its version or its health.** **NEW (22nd scan)** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.importIfRequested` opens `jdbc:sqlite:<file>` with the default (read-write) mode although its comment promises the file "is only read, never changed": opening a file with a leftover `-wal`/`-journal` lets SQLite replay or delete it, and a file that is not a Lunavera one but is a database is still touched. It checks only that a `users` table exists. It does not read `PRAGMA user_version`: a file from a **newer** app version carries columns and meanings this build ignores (they are dropped without a word), a file from before the money migration (version 0) holds `REAL` prices that MariaDB rounds into whole dong, and it does not run `PRAGMA integrity_check` / `foreign_key_check` although the foreign keys are switched off for the copy, so orphan rows (an order line whose product is gone) are copied and only fail later on the first screen that joins them. **(confirmed in code; not run, no MariaDB here)** Open the file read-only (`SQLiteConfig.setReadOnly(true)`), refuse a version above `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` with a message, warn on version 0, run `integrity_check` and `foreign_key_check` before the copy and stop with the first problem rows; test with a hand-made file for each case.

63. [ ] **[Low] A backup is never opened again after it is written, so a bad one is found only on the day it is needed.** **NEW (24th scan)** `BackupService.createBackup` takes the database copy (`VACUUM INTO`), writes the zip to `*.part` and moves it into place; nothing then reads the zip back, although the code to do so exists (`inspect`, which reads every entry so the CRCs are checked and runs `integrity_check` on the database inside). A failing disk, a USB stick pulled out just after the move or a full cache can leave a zip that looks fine in the folder; the 14 automatic copies are then all equally bad, and the first sign is the restore that fails. **(confirmed in code)** After the move, run `inspect(zip)` (a few hundred milliseconds on a shop-size file), delete and report a copy that fails ("Bản sao lưu vừa tạo bị lỗi"), and say "đã kiểm tra" in the success message; test with a zip truncated by a stand-in writer.

64. [ ] **[Low] One damaged "hire date" in a staff record stops that person logging in and empties the whole account list.** **NEW (25th scan)** `EmployeeDAO.map` reads `hire_date` with a bare `LocalDate.parse(...)` (the customer and order code use the tolerant `DbTime.parse`). A value that is not `yyyy-MM-dd` (a date typed into phpMyAdmin as `18/10/2026`, a date-time text left by an import, an empty-looking space) throws `DateTimeParseException`, which is not a `DataAccessException`: `reloadAccounts` fails with a generic error so the admin sees **no accounts at all**, and the login of that employee fails at "Không thể tải thông tin nhân viên" (`LoginController.onLoginSuccess` -> `employeeDAO.findById`), which looks like a wrong password to them. **(confirmed in code; not run)** Parse the column defensively (take the first 10 characters, return `null` and log a warning when it cannot be read), and add the three bad values to `EmployeeDAOTest`; the same few lines are worth a look in every `map` that parses text.

## 4. Features to add (most important first)

> **8th pass, status of the open features.** Done here: 4.1 (found already complete) and the `before-restore` part of 4.28. Everything else still open needs at least one of: a **new screen or dialog** (JavaFX cannot be loaded in the environment these passes run in, so a screen could not be seen or clicked), **new tables or columns in both database definitions** plus the importer, a numbered migration and tests on both databases (cannot be run there), or a **decision from the owner / an accountant**. Grouped: screens + new tables: 4.2 audit log, 4.5 stock receiving log, 4.6 shift close, 4.9 order notes, 4.10 table transfer / merge / split bill, 4.18 table management, 4.25 take-away orders, 4.31 transfer reference; screens only: 4.7 settings, 4.8 merge customers, 4.12 employee screen, 4.15 first-run wizard (the admin-account part exists, see 2A.5), 4.16 spending points, 4.19 reorder level (needs a column), 4.24 database health line, 4.26 set the transfer QR in the app, 4.27 clean up unused pictures, 4.29 former staff; owner or accountant first: 4.11 promotions, 4.22 Vietnamese invoicing rules; hardware: 4.13 kitchen ticket printer; 4.14 several tills: the MariaDB mode (1.5) is the base for it, but two tills at once have not been tried. What would let more of these be built and checked: allow `repo.maven.apache.org` in the sandbox network settings so `mvn clean test` runs, and give a way to start the app with a display (or accept that screens are checked by you).

On MariaDB the backup is the `mysqldump` file and the restore button only shows the phpMyAdmin steps (open item 1.8).

A dead disk, a stolen PC or ransomware on the till takes the data and every backup together. 5.18 asks only for a reminder at closing time. **(confirmed in code)** Add a setting "Thư mục sao lưu thứ hai" (4.7): after each automatic backup copy the zip there (a failure is shown on the dashboard, not only logged), and show "Bản sao lưu ngoài máy gần nhất: N ngày trước" with a red state after 3 days. Check the target is not inside `data/` and is writable before accepting it.

In a busy cafe two or three people share one till; they tend to stay signed in as one account, and then `orders.paid_by` (2.47) and every discount reason name the wrong person. **(reasoned)** A "Khóa" button (and the idle timeout) that locks the screen while keeping the cart, and a 4-6 digit PIN per account (stored hashed, with its own throttle) to unlock or to switch; the PIN must never be enough to sign in from the login screen, and an admin screen stays behind the password.

A shop can look profitable in the report and still lose money. **(confirmed: nothing records them)** A small `expenses` table (date, amount, category, note, who) with a screen for the administrator, the lines "Chi phí" and "Lợi nhuận ròng" in the report, and the cash taken out counted in the shift close (4.6).

The owner cannot see who has not come back for two months or who spends most, and cannot reward the top ten. **(confirmed from the table columns)** Add three columns computed in one query (`MAX(paid_at)`, `COUNT(*)`, `SUM(total)` over paid orders), sort by them, and a "Khách thân thiết" filter; the same figures help 4.16 (spending points) and 4.8 (merging customers).

The data is all there (`orders.paid_at`, `order_items` joined to `products.category_id`). **(reasoned)** Add three small queries (group by hour of `paid_at`, by weekday, by category) and a "so với kỳ trước" figure (the same number of days just before the range) to `ReportWindow`; keep them as `ReportDAO` records with tests on a fixed day, as the existing ones do. Sales by employee is item 4.6.

41. [ ] **[Med] Stock is counted in cups, not in what goes into them (beans, milk, syrup, cups, lids).** **NEW (23rd scan)** `products.stock` is one number per menu item and falls by the quantity sold, which suits a bottle of water or a slice of cake. For drinks a shop runs out of *milk* or *beans*, not of "Cà phê sữa", and one litre of milk is spread over a dozen products, so the stock column of a drink is a made-up figure (the starter menu even sets 20 for every item, 2.20). **(reasoned; no recipe or ingredient table exists)** Add `ingredients` (name, unit, stock, minimum) and `recipes` (product, ingredient, amount per serving); paying an order takes the amounts off the ingredients in the same transaction as today's stock; the low-stock card shows ingredients; a product with no recipe keeps the current cup count. Needs a migration (`SchemaMigrations`) and a screen under the admin menu.

42. [ ] **[Low] No product code or barcode, so packaged goods cannot be scanned.** **NEW (23rd scan)** `products` has a name and a category but no code. A shop that also sells bags of beans, bottled drinks or snacks has to tap each one; a USB barcode scanner types the digits and Enter like a keyboard, so it only needs a field to match. **(reasoned)** Add a nullable `code` column (unique when set) with a migration, a "Mã vạch" box in the product dialog and the CSV, and make the POS search box add the product at once when the typed text equals a code exactly (and say "không có mã này" otherwise).

43. [ ] **[Low] Table reservations.** **NEW (23rd scan)** A table is only free or taken; there is no way to mark "đặt trước 18:30, anh Nam, 4 người". On a busy evening the cashier keeps a paper list. **(reasoned)** A small `reservations` table (table, name, phone, time, people, note) shown as a badge on the table map (the dashboard and POS maps already share `DashboardWidgets`), a reminder 15 minutes before and a one-click "nhận bàn" that opens the order with the customer attached.

26. [ ] **Warn when Caps Lock is on at the login and password fields.** **NEW (8th scan)** A cashier who types the password with Caps Lock on gets "wrong password", and every wrong try also feeds the login delay (2A.7). Nothing in `src` reads the Caps Lock state (searched). JavaFX has no public call for the key's state: use AWT's `Toolkit.getLockingKeyState(KeyEvent.VK_CAPS_LOCK)` (needs the `java.desktop` module) or watch the typed letters (a capital letter without Shift); show a small "Caps Lock đang bật" line under the field.

27. [ ] **[Low] At the POS a quantity can only be changed one cup at a time.** **NEW (10th scan)** A dish card adds one (`item.setQuantity(1)`) and the "+" and "−" buttons of a line change it by one (`adjustQuantity(line, ±1)`); there is no way to type a number. A table that orders 12 cups of the same drink means 12 clicks, with the stock warning checked on each. **(confirmed in code)** Let a click on the quantity number turn it into a small number box (digits only, limited by the stock like "+" is), or add a "×N" key; keep "+" and "−" for the common case. The stock rule must stay in one function (`adjustQuantity` today) so the typed value cannot go past it.

28. [ ] **[Low] Show the points earned and the balance on the receipt.** **NEW (11th scan)** `ThermalReceipt` and the receipt window print the customer's name but not "Điểm tích lũy đơn này: +N, tổng: M", so the customer sees no sign of the loyalty programme and cannot dispute a missing point. **(confirmed in code)** `LoyaltyPolicy.pointsFor(total)` and the customer's balance are both known at payment; print them under the customer line, only when a customer is attached, and not on the transfer QR text (1.1).

29. [ ] **[Low] The phone box at the POS starts a database query at every key press from the ninth character.** **NEW (11th scan)** The listener in `OrderController.initCustomerInputs` calls `lookupCustomerByPhone` for each change of a text of 9 or more characters; a number typed as `+84 901 234 567` or pasted with spaces triggers up to seven lookups in a row, each on its own thread (`Async.run`, no ordering; the late-result guard keeps the screen right, but the work is wasted and on a MariaDB server over wifi it is seven round trips). **(confirmed in code)** Look up only when the text is a valid phone (`ValidationUtil.isValidPhone`) and wait about 250 ms after the last key (a `PauseTransition`).

30. [ ] **[Low] Saving a CSV that is still open in Excel shows only the generic error.** **NEW (12th scan)** On Windows Excel locks a file it has open, so the second "Xuất" to the same name fails with the JDK's text, which contains the file path (`C:\...: The process cannot access the file because it is being used by another process`). `ErrorText.looksTechnical` hides any text with a drive path and shows `GENERIC` ("Đã xảy ra lỗi. Hãy thử lại; nếu lỗi vẫn còn, báo người phụ trách..."), so the person is told to call someone instead of closing the file. **(the filter half is reproduced: `ErrorText.forScreen` turns the path variant into `GENERIC`, and the same text without a path is shown in English, see 2A.38; the Excel lock itself was not tried, there is no Windows here)** Catch the file-in-use case where the CSV is written (orders, customers, report: `ExportCsv.write` and the report window) and say "Không ghi được tệp. Nếu tệp đang mở trong Excel, hãy đóng nó rồi thử lại, hoặc đặt tên khác."; `FileSystemException.getReason()` or a failed `Files.newOutputStream` on an existing file is enough to tell. Related: 2A.16, 6.10.

31. [ ] **[Low] The order of categories and products on the sales screen cannot be changed: it is the order in which they were created.** **NEW (13th scan)** `CategoryDAO.findAll` ends with `ORDER BY id` and `ProductDAO` with `ORDER BY p.id`; the POS only re-sorts by best sellers (5.10) or puts out-of-stock products last. A new category (for example "Combo") is always the last tab, and the owner cannot put "Cà phê" first or move a seasonal drink to the top. **(confirmed in code)** Add a `sort_order` column (the seeded menu already has a natural order) and two arrow buttons in the category and product lists.

32. [ ] **[Low] The account text of the transfer QR (holder and bank) can only be saved together with a new picture.** **NEW (13th scan)** `AdminDashboardController.handleSetTransferQr` shows the text box, then its only OK button is "Chọn ảnh...", then the file chooser; the text is passed to `TransferQrGuard.replace` together with the picture, and cancelling the file chooser returns without saving anything. A typo in the holder's name or a changed bank cannot be corrected without choosing the picture again, and a typed text is lost when the chooser is cancelled. **(confirmed in code)** Give the dialog a second button "Chỉ lưu thông tin" (the guard already keeps the picture and its record), and keep the text when the chooser is cancelled.

33. [ ] **[Low] The money boxes refuse the way Vietnamese shops write amounts: "50k", "1tr", "50 nghìn".** **NEW (21st scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` accepts digits with dots, commas or spaces and a trailing `đ`/`d`/`VND`, so `50.000`, `50,000`, `50 000` and `50.000đ` work (all run on the real class), but `50k`, `50K`, `1tr`, `1,5tr`, `1 triệu` and `50 nghìn` all end in "Vui lòng nhập một số hợp lệ". Cashiers and owners write prices and cash received exactly like that every day (the payment dialog even has 50k / 100k / 200k buttons, item 5.1). **(reproduced by running `MoneyParser.parseDong` on 23 inputs)** Accept `k` / `nghìn` / `ngàn` (x 1,000) and `tr` / `triệu` (x 1,000,000, with a decimal part: `1,5tr` = 1,500,000) after a number, keep the digit limit (`MAX_DIGITS`) on the result, and add the 7 spellings to `MoneyParserTest`. Do not combine with a `.` thousands group (`1.500k` stays refused).

34. [ ] **[Low] The report's CSV holds only the 20 best sellers, and the window never says when it was loaded.** **NEW (22nd scan)** `ReportWindow.exportCsv` writes `lastProducts`, which is the on-screen top 20 (`TOP_PRODUCTS_LIMIT`), under the heading "Sản phẩm": an accountant who exports a month for a 60-product menu gets a list that silently ends at 20, and the file has no note that it is a top list. The window also keeps the figures of the moment "Xem báo cáo" was pressed: left open during the day it goes stale with no "cập nhật lúc HH:mm" stamp and no refresh when an order is paid. **(confirmed in code)** Export all products (a second query without the limit), or at least write "Top 20" in the heading; show the load time next to the summary and reload when the window gets focus again.

35. [ ] **[Low] The customer search box ignores the email, although the email column is shown.** **NEW (22nd scan)** `CustomerController.applyFilter` compares the typed text with the name and the phone only. A cashier who knows only the address a customer gave cannot find them, and typing part of an email returns an empty list that looks like "no such customer". **(confirmed in code)** Add the e-mail (through `TextNormalizer.searchKey`) to the filter, and say in the box's prompt text what it searches ("Tên, số điện thoại hoặc email").

36. [ ] **[Low] The receipt, history and report windows have no owner window.** **NEW (23rd scan)** `SceneNavigator.loadAndShowNewWindow`, `ReportWindow.open` and `OrderHistoryWindow` each create a plain `new Stage()` without `initOwner`. On Windows each one gets its own taskbar button and a click on the main window puts it in front of them, so the receipt that just opened for a payment can end up hidden behind the till with nothing on screen to say it is still there (the cashier thinks the print did not open and pays or prints again). The windows are also not closed when the main window is, except on logout (2.30). **(confirmed in code that no owner is set; the stacking is the usual Windows behaviour, not seen here)** Pass the main stage as owner (`stage.initOwner(Session/main window)`), keep them non-modal, and test by hand on Windows: open a receipt, click the till, the receipt must stay in front.

37. [ ] **[Med] On a shop with two or more tills the POS never refreshes its table map or its stock numbers.** **NEW (25th scan)** The dashboards reload every 60 s (`AutoRefresh`), but `OrderController` loads the dining tables and the product list once when the screen opens and has no timer except the clock. A table that the other till just opened still shows free here, a table paid there still shows taken, and a product that sold out at the other till still shows its old stock; the database refuses the wrong action (3.33, `rejectIfTableHasOpenOrder`, the stock check at payment), but only after the cashier has built an order and pressed the button, with a message that blames neither till. Several tills on one MariaDB server are a supported set-up. **(confirmed in code: no refresh in `OrderController`; the effect needs two tills)** Add a refresh of the tables and the stock every 15-20 s while the screen is shown and no payment is being saved (`AutoRefresh.every`, results through `Async.run` and the late-answer guard of 2.55), keep the selected table, and show "cập nhật lúc HH:mm" in the corner.

## 6. Practices to adopt (most important first)

       workflow that runs `mvn test`. *(8th pass: DONE as far as the code goes. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `mvn -B test` on Ubuntu and Windows with JDK 21 on every push and pull request, and `.github/dependabot.yml` exists. Checked by reading: no test starts a JavaFX toolkit (so no display is needed on the CI machines), `mvnw` is executable, `.gitattributes` keeps `mvnw` on LF line endings. Not run yet: it runs the first time you push. What stays yours: committing often and tagging each release, e.g. `git tag v0.9`.)*
2. [x] **Add a `README.md`.** **NEW (2nd scan)** The project root has only `TODO.md` and `progress.md`. Write: what the app is, how to run (`mvn javafx:run`), how to run the tests, where the data folder is (1.3),
       the first-run login and that the password must be changed, how to back up and restore, and a short map of the folders (see the architecture notes). *(8th pass: DONE. The README already had what the app is, how to run and test, the first-run account (there is no built-in password; old default passwords must be changed), where the data is, backup and restore and the database set-up; this pass added the missing map of the folders and a line about the CI. Checked against the code: the backup is automatic once a day, the newest 14 kept.)*

By a new search `DashboardStatsService` was the one class from this list with no test at all; `DashboardStatsServiceTest` (10) now covers the warning texts (5, run here without a database: pass) and the figures on the real database (5, NOT run): a paid order moves today's orders, revenue and the paid count, an open order counts but adds no revenue, a refund removes revenue and counts as cancelled, a nearly empty product counts as low stock, and the 7-day series has seven days with today last. `ReportDAO` is covered by `Section4ReportsTest`, `EmployeeDAO` by `EmployeeDetailsTest` and `StaffPhoneUniqueTest`.        `Async` (the `runOrdered` ordering guarantee the autosave depends on), `TextLengthLimiter`, `PageBar`, `PasswordReveal` or `MenuSeedData`. Start with `ReportDAO`,
       `DashboardStatsService` and `Async.runOrdered`. **(confirmed by name search)** *(8th pass: PART DONE. Added: `MenuSeedDataTest` (5 tests, run with a stand-in runner against the real data and picture files: unique names, sane prices, every picture bundled with a safe file name, no picture used twice) and `CheckoutServiceTest` (see 6.5). What is still without a test is only the part that needs a JavaFX toolkit: `PasswordReveal`, the controls of `PageBar` and the wiring in `IdleLogout`; they are tracked in 6.35 (TestFX with Monocle, or a small seam). 4. [x] **Split `AdminController`** (1,367 lines serving four screens) into one controller per screen, and extract a shared base class for the repeated navigation *(13th pass, part 4: DONE, ticked. `AdminController` (1,793 lines, four screens in one class, `if (x != null)` guards everywhere) is gone. 24. [x] **[Med] Add CHECK constraints to the schema.** **NEW (5th scan)** The tables have only `NOT NULL`/`UNIQUE`/foreign keys **(confirmed from the CREATE TABLE text)**. Nothing in the database refuses a negative price or stock, an order item with quantity 0 or less, a discount above the subtotal, or a status such as `'paid'` that no code understands. *(13th pass: **SQLite is done**: `SchemaMigrations.ensureDataRules` creates insert and update triggers that refuse a negative price, cost or stock, an order line with quantity 0 or less or negative money, an order with negative money or a discount above the subtotal, an unknown order status, an unknown user role or status, an unknown table status, and negative loyalty points. Triggers instead of `CHECK` because SQLite cannot add a `CHECK` to an existing table without rebuilding it, and one mechanism then covers new and old databases. The allowed values are written from the enums (`OrderStatus`, `Role`, `AccountStatus`, `TableStatus`), so they cannot drift. Safe at every start (`IF NOT EXISTS`); a rule is skipped, with a warning that gives only the rule and a row count plus the query that lists the rows, while rows already in the database break it (otherwise every later update of such a row would fail), and quietly when its table or column is missing. I read the code first so no real flow trips a rule: stock is only decreased with `stock >= quantity`, loyalty points are clamped at 0, a paid order is written in one statement that sets subtotal, discount and total together. The rules were run in Python's SQLite on the real `CREATE TABLE` text with a normal sale, a cancel, a restock and a loyalty clamp (all pass) and ten bad writes (all refused). `DataRulesTest` (9; NOT run, needs the driver). The MariaDB half was added in part 2 below; one rule is not a rule on either database: `total = subtotal - discount` for a paid order (the code checks it; a trigger would need to be sure no draft order breaks it).)* *(13th pass, part 2: DONE for both databases, so ticked. MariaDB/MySQL now gets the same rules as `CHECK` constraints: `SchemaMigrations.ensureMariaDataRules` runs at every start next to the staff-phone key, skips a rule that already exists (looked up in `information_schema.table_constraints`), skips a rule with a warning (rule name and row count only) while existing rows break it, and tries each rule on its own, so a failure (an old server that cannot add a `CHECK`, a lost connection) is logged and never stops the app from starting. The statement is `ALTER TABLE <t> ADD CONSTRAINT rule_<name> CHECK (NOT (<violation>))`, built by `mariaCheckSql`; `DataRuleSqlTest` (4, run here: pass) fixes its shape and checks that every column the rules name exists in both schemas. **NOT run on a MariaDB server**: the syntax is the documented one for MariaDB 10.2+ and MySQL 8.0.16+, and a rule that fails only produces a warning in the log, so look for \"Data rule ... was not added\" in `data/logs/` on the first start and, in phpMyAdmin, `SHOW CREATE TABLE products` should list `CONSTRAINT rule_products_not_negative CHECK ...`. A server older than MySQL 8.0.16 parses the `CHECK` and ignores it, and gets the statement again at each start. Still not a rule, on either database: `total = subtotal - discount` for a paid order (the code checks it).)*
        Add `CHECK (price >= 0)`, `CHECK (stock >= 0)`, `CHECK (quantity > 0)`, `CHECK (status IN ('OPEN','PAID','CANCELLED'))` and similar. SQLite cannot add a CHECK to an existing table, so this needs the "create new table, copy, drop, rename" migration (see 6.6), done with a backup first and tested on an old database.

The subtotal for the rule is the sum of unit price x quantity of the lines, not the figure the screen sends. A missing role counts as an ordinary employee. The reason is saved as `DiscountPolicy.reasonToStore` gives it (trimmed). `DiscountPolicy` gained `messageFor(result, role, subtotal)` (the dialog now uses it, same words) and `require`, which throws a `DataAccessException` and logs only the result name. `OrderController.completeOrder` reads `Session.getCurrentRole()` on the screen thread and passes it, so a role demoted while the dialog was open is judged at payment time. Tests: 6 new in `CheckoutServiceTest` (staff over the limit refused with no customer and no stock taken; within the limit saved with the trimmed reason; admin large discount but a reason is still needed; no role = staff limit; a lying screen subtotal is not trusted; a refused discount leaves the open order open and a legal retry pays it) and 5 in `DiscountPolicyTest` (15 of 15 pass in a stand-in runner). Not covered: a screen that calls `OrderDAO.payExistingOrder` or `insert` directly still bypasses the rule, because the DAO does not know who is signed in; today only `CheckoutService` does. NOT compiled: the `OrderController` edit (needs JavaFX); the new `CheckoutServiceTest` tests need the database.)*






---

46. [ ] **[Low] Success callbacks of background jobs run without any safety net.** **NEW (8th scan)** `Async.newTask` does `task.setOnSucceeded(e -> onSuccess.accept(task.getValue()))`. If the callback throws (a missing label, a window that fails to open), the exception goes to the global handler and the rest of the callback never runs, which is how 2.65 can leave a paid order on screen. **(confirmed in code)** Wrap the callback in `try/catch`, log it, and show one "Đã lưu, nhưng màn hình không cập nhật được: hãy mở lại màn hình" message.

47. [ ] **[Low] Run the MariaDB tests once with `sql_mode=ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY`.** **NEW (8th scan)** `ReportDAO.topProducts` selects `COALESCE(p.name, oi.product_name)` but groups by `COALESCE(CAST(oi.product_id AS CHAR), oi.product_name)`. SQLite and the default MariaDB mode accept it; MySQL, and a MariaDB server whose `sql_mode` includes `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY`, refuse it with error 1055, and the report would fail on that shop's server. **(reasoned from the query; the 415 MariaDB test runs used the default mode)** Add the flag to the test server's settings and wrap the column in `MIN()`/`MAX()` or group by it.

48. [ ] **[Low] Use `MessageDigest.isEqual` for the hash comparison.** **NEW (8th scan)** `PasswordUtil.constantTimeEquals` is a hand-written loop. It is correct, but the JDK already ships the same thing, and a hand-written security routine is one more thing to keep tested. **(confirmed in code)**

49. [ ] **[Low] The project has no LICENSE file and no CHANGELOG.** **NEW (9th scan)** The root holds `README.md`, `TODO.md`, `progress.md` and `docs/`, but no `LICENSE` (so, by default, nobody else may legally use or change the code, and a contributor or the next owner cannot tell) and no `CHANGELOG.md` (the shop has no list of what each tagged version changed, which `RELEASE.md` step 4 would use when the old version has to be kept for a week). **(confirmed: `ls` of the project root)** Decide the licence with the owner (or write "all rights reserved" on purpose in the README) and start `CHANGELOG.md` with one section per `git tag`.

50. [ ] **[Low] `AppClock` is called "the one place" that reads the time, but data and default dates still read the system clock in several places.** **NEW (9th scan)** Besides the allowed ones (idle timer, crash-dialog spacing, file names), `OrderController` saves the opening time of a new order with `LocalDateTime.now()` (line 789, written to `orders.order_date`) and builds the 30-day best-seller range from `LocalDate.now()` (line 421); `DatePresets` (line 45), `ReportWindow` (its two date pickers), `FirstRunDialog` (the owner's hire date), `LunaveraApplication` (line 135) and `BackupService.pendingIsOlderThan` do the same. In the app the result is the same, but a test that pins the date with `AppClock.pinDate` cannot reach these, and the order-opening time is the one value that the rest of the data code (`paid_at`, `EFFECTIVE_DAY`) takes from `AppClock`. **(confirmed by grep)** Fix: use `AppClock.now()`/`today()` in these places and update the class comment, or keep a short list of the allowed exceptions in it.

51. [ ] **[Low] The CI workflow has no safety settings, and the tests are run differently from the release checklist.** **NEW (10th scan)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has no `permissions:` block (so the job token gets the repository's default rights, which can include write access), no `timeout-minutes` (a hung test runs for six hours), no `concurrency` (a new push does not stop the old run), its actions are named by tag (`actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-java@v4`) and not by commit, it runs `./mvnw -B test` while `docs/RELEASE.md` says `clean test`, it does not keep the Surefire reports when a test fails, and it never builds the package, so a broken jlink setting (6.15) shows up only on release day. **(confirmed from the file)** Add `permissions: contents: read`, `timeout-minutes: 20`, `concurrency` with `cancel-in-progress`, pin the actions to commit hashes (the Dependabot entry for `github-actions` already keeps them current), use `clean test`, upload `target/surefire-reports` when the job fails, and run `./mvnw -B -DskipTests package` once on Linux.

52. [ ] **[Low] More comments describe the default administrator that no longer exists.** **NEW (10th scan)** Extends 6.43. The Javadoc of `DatabaseConfig.initialize` still says it "seeds one default admin account the very first time", a comment at line 309 says "Before the default admin is seeded", `LunaveraApplication` (line 57) says that on a first run "the default admin account is seeded", and the seeding method logs "Seeded default admin account ... Change this password after first login". Since 2A.5 the first start asks the owner to create the administrator instead. **(confirmed in code)** Rewrite the three comments to describe the first-run screen; the log line goes with 2A.33.

53. [ ] **[Low] The packaged program is called "app".** **NEW (10th scan)** The jlink settings in `pom.xml` (lines 137-139) set `<launcher>app</launcher>`, `<jlinkZipName>app</jlinkZipName>` and `<jlinkImageName>app</jlinkImageName>`, so the shop unzips a folder `app` with a starter `bin/app` (or `app.bat`), nothing says Lunavera, and there is no Start-menu entry or icon (a jlink image is only a folder; `jpackage` makes the Windows installer or an `.exe` with an icon, the project does not use it). **(confirmed from the pom)** Name all three `lunavera`, and when 6.15 is done try `jpackage --type msi` (or `app-image`) with the icon of 4.17, so the owner double-clicks a shortcut. Write the result in `docs/PACKAGING.md`.

54. [ ] **[Low] The "no controller over 900 lines" rule covers only the admin screens.** **NEW (11th scan)** `AdminScreensTest.noControllerIsAMonster` checks the five admin controllers; `OrderController` is 1,365 lines (the biggest file in the project), `OrderHistoryWindow` 379, and the sidebar navigation and `logout` are still copied into `OrderController`, `CustomerController` and `EmployeeController` (6.4 says so). **(counted)** Extend the test to every class in `controller/` with a ceiling per file that can only go down (a small map: `OrderController` 1,400 today), so growth fails the build and each refactor lowers the number; make the three remaining screens extend a shared navigation base (not necessarily `AdminScreen`, which also has `requireAdmin`).

55. [ ] **[Low] `Messages.format` formats numbers with the PC's language, not Vietnamese.** **NEW (11th scan)** `Messages.get` always uses the `vi` bundle, but `Messages.format` hands the text to `MessageFormat.format`, which formats a number argument with the JVM's default locale: `1234` becomes `1,234` on an English Windows and `1.234` on a Vietnamese one, in the same sentence that `CurrencyUtil` writes with dots. No screen calls `format` yet (only `CategoryDialog` uses `get`), so it is latent; it appears the moment the product count or an amount goes through it (6.10). **(confirmed in code)** Build the `MessageFormat` with `Locale.forLanguageTag("vi")` (or write amounts through `CurrencyUtil` first and pass text), and add a case to `MessagesTest` that runs under a different default locale (the way `ReceiptQrTest` already does).

56. [ ] **[Low] A little dead code, and a style class that no style sheet defines.** **NEW (12th scan)** `BackupService.listBackups()` is public and has no caller in the program or in the tests; `DatabaseConfig.describeConnection()` is called only by `TestDatabaseSupport`; `quanlysanpham.fxml` line 143 gives an `HBox` the style class `product-area`, which no style sheet defines (it was not defined before the 13th-pass clean-up either), and `CssRulesTest` only checks the other direction (rules nothing uses). **(confirmed by search)** Delete the two unused methods (or use `listBackups` for the restore picker of 4.35), drop or define `product-area`, and extend `CssRulesTest` so that a style class used in an FXML file but defined in no style sheet fails the test, except the built-in JavaFX classes.

57. [ ] **[Low] `Messages.get` logs a missing key every time it is asked, although its comment says once.** **NEW (13th scan)** The class comment says a missing key "logs it once"; the code calls `LOGGER.warning("Missing text key: " + key)` on every call. A key that is missing from `messages_vi.properties` and used in a loop, a table cell or a timer fills the 3 x 1 MB log (2A.25) in minutes. `MessagesTest` finds a typo at test time, but not a key removed later from the file. **(confirmed in code)** Keep a `Set<String>` of the keys already reported, or fix the comment.

58. [ ] **[Low] The README tells people to run `mvn`, but the project ships a pinned Maven wrapper and the CI and `docs/RELEASE.md` use it.** **NEW (14th scan)** `README.md` ("Run and test") says `mvn javafx:run` and `mvn test`; the word `mvnw` does not appear in it (counted). A shop owner or a new helper without Maven installed cannot follow it, and a different Maven version on the machine can behave differently from the 3.8.5 that the CI runs. **(confirmed in the file)** Write `./mvnw javafx:run` and `./mvnw clean test` (`mvnw.cmd` on Windows) and keep `mvn` only as a footnote.

59. [ ] **[Low] No code-coverage report, so "which classes have no test" is found by hand.** **NEW (14th scan)** `pom.xml` has no JaCoCo (or any coverage) plugin; item 6.3 found the untested classes by reading, and each new test round has to do it again. **(confirmed: not in the pom, not in the CI)** Add `jacoco-maven-plugin` with a `report` goal bound to `verify`, upload the HTML report from the CI run, and look at it once before deciding on any minimum (no failing gate at the start; the JavaFX controllers will show as uncovered).

60. [ ] **[Low] `QrCodeTest` checks one symbol of version 1; the other 39 versions are untested.** **NEW (16th scan)** `matchesIndependentReferenceEncoder` compares one text (version 1, 21x21) with stored modules; the other tests check sizes, finder patterns and timing. Alignment patterns (version 2 and up), the version information (7 and up) and the interleaving of several Reed-Solomon blocks (version 5 and up) are never looked at, and the receipt QR switches between versions with the length of the text. **To settle it I decoded the output independently:** 86 texts of 1 to 2,331 bytes (every capacity boundary of the table, one real receipt text, one Vietnamese text) were encoded and then read back by a separate Python decoder (format information and its BCH check, both copies; mask; zig-zag read; de-interleaving; Reed-Solomon check of every block; byte-mode payload). **All 86 verified, in all 40 versions, and the two ECC tables equal the standard ones** - so the encoder is right today; nothing protects it from a change. Add a test-side `QrDecoder` (about 120 lines: the same steps) and a test that round-trips a text of each version-changing length, plus one with a text of more than 255 bytes (16-bit count field from version 10). Keep it in `src/test` only.
61. [ ] **[Low] Every test run leaves a 3 MB folder behind in the system temp folder.** **NEW (17th scan)** `TestDatabaseSupport` creates `lunavera-dao-test-home*` with `Files.createTempDirectory` in its static block and never deletes it (no `deleteOnExit`, no shutdown hook; the class comment even says there is "no delete-after-test bookkeeping"). The folder holds the data folder with the starter menu photos copied into it, about 3 MB. **Reproduced:** about a hundred runs of my test and probe programs left about a hundred such folders (300 MB) in `/tmp` until the sandbox disk was full; with Maven it is one folder per `mvn test`, which `mvn clean` does not remove (it is outside `target/`) and which on Windows piles up in `%TEMP%`. Fix: register a shutdown hook that deletes `TEMP_HOME` recursively, or create it under `target/` (where `pom.xml` already points `lunavera.dataDir` and `user.home`, a setting this class overrides).
62. [ ] **[Low] Digits written with `String.format` follow the PC's regional settings, and 8 tests fail under an Arabic locale.** **NEW (18th scan)** `String.format("%d")` and `%02d` use the digits of the default locale. **Reproduced:** with `-Duser.language=ar -Duser.country=SA` the whole suite gives 568 passed and 8 failed (`Section4ReportsTest`, `AccountServiceTest` x2, `CheckoutServiceTest`, `BackupPruneTest` x2, `BackupServiceTest`, and the known 3.42), because the tests build phone numbers and backup file names with 11 unqualified `String.format` calls and get Arabic-Indic digits (`\u0660`...). In the app itself the same call builds the bank-transfer note in `OrderController` (`"DH" + String.format("%06d", id)`, shown to the customer, so `DH\u0660\u0660...` on such a PC) and the three "Hiển thị a – b / c" labels (`CustomerController`, `AccountController`, `CategoryController`). The shop's own settings (vi_VN with Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh) and Turkish with a UTC+14 time zone both pass (575 passed, only 3.42 fails), so this is a practice item: pass `Locale.ROOT` to every `String.format`, move the transfer note into `OrderNumber` (see 3.56), and add `-Duser.language=en` (or a second CI run under `ar_SA`) to the surefire settings so a locale change cannot hide behind the PC that runs the tests.

63. [ ] **[Low] The loyalty tiers live in a controller, and the policy class points to it.** **NEW (22nd scan)** `LoyaltyPolicy` holds the points-per-dong rule, but the thresholds (100 = Bạc, 500 = Vàng) are in `CustomerController.tierFor`, and the javadoc of `LoyaltyPolicy` says so. The rule is therefore untestable without JavaFX, cannot be used by the receipt (5.28) or a customer export, and a change in one place is easy to forget in the other. **(confirmed in code)** Move `tierFor` into `LoyaltyPolicy` (`Tier tierFor(int points)` with the two thresholds as constants), call it from the controller, and add the boundary cases (0, 99, 100, 499, 500) to `LoyaltyPolicyTest`; item 4.x (configurable tiers) can then change one class.

64. [ ] **[Low] `SceneNavigator.switchScene` and `switchSceneAndGetController` are the same twenty lines.** **NEW (23rd scan)** Both check the login target, run `SessionGuard.validateNow()`, load the FXML, call `showScreen` and handle the `IOException`; only the return value differs, and the second has already drifted (it returns `null` after a forced logout, the first returns nothing). A fix to one (for example a loading indicator, or the DB check moving off the screen thread, 6.14) has to be made twice. **(confirmed in code)** Make the first call the second and ignore the result; keep one test that a locked account is refused on both.

## 8. Done (for reference)
Quality of life (cleanup doc, section 4) items 1-10 are done. Items 6-9 (log no longer prints the default password, LIKE escaping in the order
history search, `orders.table_number` in the CREATE TABLE plus a checked add for old databases, the `LoginBackground.png` rename) were done after items 1-5;
see progress.md Session 33. Cleanup list steps 1-3 (dead code, unused dependencies, bugs) are done as well.

**Checked in the 3rd scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- No SQL is built from user input: every value goes through a `PreparedStatement` parameter. The few concatenated statements use fixed table names or ids read from the database itself.
- Password verification compares the hashes in constant time, a nonexistent username costs one hash, and the locked-account message only appears after the right password.
- Money is stored as whole dong (integer), foreign keys are on, and each multi-step write (payment, cancel, cleanup) runs in one transaction.
- The report window and the backup button check for an admin account; cancelling a paid order is admin-only.
- Passwords are never written to the log (the default admin password line was removed).

**Checked in the 4th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- User-entered text never reaches a file path, an FXML file or a style sheet; the export file name is built from dates only.
- The report window is admin-only and the account lock refuses to lock the signed-in account (the last-admin race is item 2A.2).
- The report export uses a BOM and `\r\n`, so Excel shows Vietnamese correctly.

**Checked in the 5th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- `VACUUM INTO` gets its path with single quotes doubled, so a folder name with an apostrophe cannot break or inject into the statement (the other problems with `backupTo` are bug 2.23).
- Payment is guarded against double payment: `payExistingOrder` only updates a row that is still `OPEN`, and a second attempt gets a clear message and changes nothing. Stock cannot go below zero (`stock >= ?` in the same transaction).
- Cancelling a paid order restocks, takes back points and changes the status in one transaction and rolls back on any error.
- The idle `Async` threads are daemons, the ordered queue is one thread (autosave then payment cannot overtake each other), and the ordered-queue name in the code is `runOrdered`.
- No log line I found writes a phone number, a name or a password.
- Not scanned in depth in this round (so no claim either way): the CSS files, the layout of every FXML file, `OrderHistoryWindow` and `ReportWindow` beyond what earlier scans covered.


**Checked in the 6th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- Every `onAction`/`onMouse...="#handler"` in the nine FXML files has a matching method in its controller (0 missing); every `<stylesheets>` and image path in the FXML files points to a file that exists.
- Deleting a product that has sales is blocked by the screen with a clear offer to stop selling it instead (`countOrderReferences`); the DAO alone would fail with a foreign-key error (reproduced: "Failed to delete product"), which is the intended last defence.
- Customer phone numbers are normalised inside `CustomerDAO` (insert, update, `existsByPhone`): `+84912345678` was stored as `0912345678`, so the customer dialog cannot create the same person under two spellings.
- The login screen runs the password check off the UI thread, trims only the username (not the password) and shows one message for a wrong name or password.
- The history search escapes `%` and `_`, sorting and paging happen in SQL, and the cancel button is re-checked for admin on click (not only when the row is selected).
- The report export writes a BOM and `\r\n` and shows the saved path; the report and its totals are admin-only.
- What I could not do: view any screen (no display here), so the visual look of every FXML/CSS screen, the 1366x768 fit, and the Windows glyphs remain for the human checks in section 0.


**Checked in the 7th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- Restore cannot write outside the pictures folder: `extractImages` (called `replaceImages` before the 2.42 fix) normalises every zip entry path and skips one that does not start with `images/`. *(25th scan: re-read, the check `file.startsWith(root)` is there.)*
- A backup is written to `<name>.zip.part` and moved into place at the end, always ends in `.zip` (so it can never be `lunavera.db`), and automatic pruning touches only `auto-*.zip`; this matches what 2.8 says.
- Every SQL value in the DAOs I read (users, orders, customers, products, employees, tables, report) goes through a `PreparedStatement` parameter; the history `ORDER BY` is a whitelist; the few concatenated statements use fixed names or ids read from the database.
- Login: a nonexistent username costs one hash (`dummyHash`), a locked account is reported only after the right password, and `PasswordChangeService` checks the current password before a voluntary change.
- Payment: the discount rule is checked in the dialog and again by `DiscountPolicy`, a transfer payment cannot be completed without the tick box, and stock cannot go below zero inside the payment transaction.
- Idle logout closes every other window and the forced password dialog (hiding it ends the session cleanly).
- Read in a second round: `CategoryDAO`, `MenuSeeder`, `ScreenFit`, `CustomerController`'s dialog. Still not scanned in depth: the CSS and FXML layouts, `QrCode` internals, `MenuSeedData`, `PagedTable`/`PageBar`/`DashboardWidgets`, and the content of the tests (only their names were compared with the classes).
